# BRL experiment and operations ledger

## Evidence rules, from October 8, 2026, 10:45 p.m. Eastern (after an outside review)

1. **Development evidence and untouched evidence are kept apart.** The 2023 to 2026 regular seasons have all been used
   to choose settings, so their replays are development evidence for the system as a whole, including 2026 (the player
   model's coefficients never saw 2026, but the settings around it were picked on it). Untouched evidence is only what
   was not yet played when a version was fixed: the live forecasts saved before first pitch, from the 2026 postseason on.
   Every version is identified by the code commit in force at a game's first pitch, and its untouched record counts only
   games after that commit. The version in force at this rule's writing is the commit that adds it.
2. **Only forward-in-time results decide.** A replay of an earlier season with tables fitted on a later one (2025 with
   2026 fits) shows whether a setting carries over between seasons; it is reported as that and does not count toward
   adopting anything registered after this rule. Rules already registered (PROD-03, LIVE-05, DISP-01, RUNS-03) are
   applied as written, with this caveat stated in their results.
3. **A decision states which gate it passed.** A change adopted on a point estimate whose interval includes zero is
   adopted as a product call and says so; it is never described as passing a stricter gate. Before a rule is written,
   the noise of each criterion is computed (game sampling and simulation noise), and thresholds sit outside it.
4. **Words match measures.** "Better than a coin flip" is the Brier skill score (how much smaller the win-chance error
   is than always saying 50-50), not the share of winners picked; both are reported. The line-movement result (CLV-02)
   compares a forecast that includes posted lineups with an opening line that usually does not, so it shows the market
   catching up to information our number had, not an early edge; a test with only what was known at the open is CLV-03.
   Pitches shown in simulated games are borrowed from real at-bats that ended the same way and decide nothing.
5. **Data definitions across seasons are checked before seasons are pooled.** From 2026, plate_x and plate_z are measured
   at the middle of the plate (front of the plate through 2025) and sz_top and sz_bot are the ABS zone (Statcast CSV
   documentation). Any feature or test that compares pitch location or zone membership across that boundary is audited
   (ZONE-01) before it is trusted.
6. **Added October 9, 2026, after an outside audit (AUDIT-01).** August 1 to September 27, 2026 is no longer called
   untouched for the matchup program: the aiming edge was re-priced there (VALUE-01F and VALUE-02F withdrawn, VALUE-08F
   on the same months) and earlier horizon work inspected 2026. Rows that scored those months are later-period
   validation with adaptive reuse; a new registration cannot restore an inspected holdout. Forward confirmation blocks
   are the 2026 postseason and 2027, with predeclared stopping (a fixed horizon or a confidence sequence, never the
   first favorable interval). Three forecast streams are kept apart in every record: the simulator (results only), our
   model (weights taught on past closing lines; never the current line) and the headline (with the current line). Run
   values from the natural experiment are estimated policy potential, not observed runs saved, and say so. Backfilled
   reports are retrospective reconstructions (earlier data, later method). Monte Carlo error (10,000 worlds: about
   0.5 points at 50%) is reported apart from game-sampling uncertainty.

## Checkpoint, October 8, 2026, 6 a.m. Eastern

Production simulator (pa-2026-v2-physics with ADJUST in brl_live/boxscore.py): context offsets, run environment (ENV-03),
team offsets centered on the league (TEAM-05, CENTER-01), base running (RUN-01, RUN-02), postseason starter usage.
Headline: taught-v4 from October 8, 14:00 UTC (HEADLINE-04): 60% market line, 40% our model (0.039 + 0.543 simulator
log-odds + 0.545 team-model log-odds).

| Season (full replay, 1,000 worlds, same seeds) | Simulator before tonight | Simulator now | Closing line | Headline (each season taught on the other) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026, 2,454 games (out of sample) | 0.24405 | 0.24378 | 0.24326 | 0.24317 |
| 2025, 1,686 games with a closing line (player model tuned on 2025; read the changes, not the level) | 0.24448 | 0.24253 | 0.24235 | 0.24150 |

Simulator correlation with the closing log-odds 0.82 to 0.87 (2026); mean total runs 8.96 against 8.95 actual (2026),
8.89 against 8.90 (2025). Not switched: the retrained player model (RETRAIN-02). Discovery: DISC-01 to DISC-05.

Later on October 8: in-game win chance averaged with the league table (LIVE-04, both seasons); pitcher-level tunneling measured and found descriptive only (DISC-06); starter projections scored (PLAYER-01). Afternoon: base running after each outcome now comes from every 2023-2026 play (TRANS-01: batters per game at the real number, win chances better in both seasons); context offsets re-estimated on the simulator as it runs (CTX-02: strikeout level and starters' strikeout projections fixed); feeding the model every physics season since 2023 was tested and rejected (SKEW-01, SKEW-02: right level, worse totals); role offsets superseded (ROLE-01).

Standing goals (Alec): the best simulator and page; continual evaluation and improvement, every change replayed on full seasons before it goes live; a scouting focus; measuring things not measured before, with predictions written before each run and failures kept. Nothing new is made public without Alec's approval (the discovery page is private).

## Checkpoint — October 7, 2026 (Claude takes over the project)

Evidence base: a full out-of-sample replay of the 2026 regular season with the locked PA model and fitted starter hazard, every input prior-date (lineups and starters as they actually played, bullpens from the team's last 14 days, roles from the prior 365 days), 2,427 games, 200 worlds each, finite-path corrected. Scores are winner Brier (coin flip = 0.25000); "better than coin" = (0.25 - Brier) / 0.25. Fair team model: negative-binomial runs with decayed, shrunk offense/defense ratings and prior-data home field, tuned on 2025 only (tau = 180 days, k = 15).

| DISC-01 | Decision horizon (discovery/DECISION_HORIZON_PROTOCOL.md, frozen before results; tools/brl_discovery.py): when MLB hitters stop using the ball's flight for swing decisions, from 2.8 million rebuilt pitches 2023-2026 | Within-type movement surprise with pitch-type location maps: 262 ms before the plate on 2026 (tau squared 0.0664 to 0.0702), 260 ms on 2025, 259 ms on primary fastballs; same sideways and vertically (258, 264), flat from 79 to 97 mph (fixed time, not distance), every count, inning and pitch type; fit turns back by 400 ms. Type level: gravity-only projection from 225 ms explains swings 40 nats per 1,000 decisions better than the true crossing. Contact (launch angle on balls in play): bat steered until about 100 to 125 ms (assumed geometry). Pre-run rule wanted 100-250 ms: recorded as not a clean pass; all later predictions met. **Continue; next: score pitch-pair tunneling at 260 ms against 175 ms.** | measurement |

Tracking error is ruled out as the source of the horizon; umpires are not fooled by late movement. **All three predictions held.** | research |

| DISC-07 | Ball-strike challenges as a second decision about the same pitch (discovery/DECISION_HORIZON_PROTOCOL.md addendum 13, committed before the run; run 37836217655): every 2026 player challenge on a called pitch, by who made it, through the umpire-test instrument | **Registered before the run.** Predictions: batters' decisions to challenge called strikes follow where the pitch looked about 260 ms out (tau squared 0.02 to 0.06 after the method's measured attenuation, interval above zero; synthetic: 0.034 at 260 ms, 0.004 on the crossing); catchers' challenges of called balls follow the crossing (interval including zero, estimate below 0.015). Results (370,808 called pitches; 3,468 batter, 4,294 catcher and 133 pitcher challenges): batters 0.0044 (0.0009 to 0.0079), the held-out fit best at the crossing and 3.5 nats per 1,000 worse at the swing horizon's value, the size synthetic batters judging the crossing give (0.004); catchers -0.0056 (-0.0081 to -0.0032), the synthetic null's value. Prediction 1 failed, prediction 2 held in size. **Reading: the horizon is when the swing is committed, not a limit on what hitters can know: taking a pitch, the same hitters judge its crossing as well as catchers (from watching it in or from the catch; not separable here).** Overturn rates: batters 57.8%, catchers 55.3%, pitchers 39.9%. | research |

Pitcher: surprise stable (0.75 year to year) but predicts walks (+0.32 pts per SD) and fewer strikes, not chases or whiffs. Familiarity: horizon 259, 263, 268 ms first to third time through, flat with pitch-type exposure. Per-hitter steering limit (run 37743322069): split-half 0.18, under the 0.2 set in advance; the apparent late steerers are power hitters whose misses leave the ball-in-play sample. **All four dropped.** | none |
| DISC-08 | Running low on ball-strike challenges (discovery/DECISION_HORIZON_PROTOCOL.md addendum 14, committed before the run; practitioner input: hitters down to their last challenge get hesitant and the zone widens on them). Every 2026 called pitch of innings 1-9 with each team's challenges left before it (two minus its failed challenges): challenge odds with one left against two at the same location against the batter's zone, count, inning and hand, with the batter's own habit and with the other team's challenges left as a placebo; overturn rates and clear misses left standing by challenges left; swings near the edge of the zone by challenges left | **Registered before the run.** Predictions: batters' odds ratio 0.45 to 0.80 with the interval excluding 1; their overturn rate at least 5 points higher with one left, and more clear misses left standing; catchers 0.45 to 0.90, interval excluding 1; placebo 0.85 to 1.15 with the interval including 1; two-strike swings near the edge up with no challenge left (1.00 to 1.25) and with one left (1.00 to 1.10); in the ninth, batters challenge clear misses with one left at least as often as with two. Falsifier: batters' odds ratio at or above 0.90 or an interval including 1. Results (run 37852742097; 714,483 pitches, 3,378 batter and 4,207 catcher challenges, 200 bootstrap draws): batters 0.72 (0.66 to 0.78), with the batter's own habit 0.71 (0.65 to 0.79); catchers 0.47 (0.42 to 0.51); clear misses left standing 77.8% to 81.6% (batters) and 62.1% to 75.3% (catchers, called balls inside the zone) with one left against two; overturn rate of the challenges made 59.1% to 53.8% (batters) and 55.8% to 53.7% (catchers), lower, not higher; placebo 1.11 (1.02 to 1.22) and 1.13 (1.05 to 1.23), so a team challenges a little more when the other team is down, and the own effect is unchanged with the placebo in the model (0.71, 0.46); two-strike swings near the edge unchanged (one left 0.99, 0.93 to 1.05; none left 1.01, 0.91 to 1.13); in the ninth, clear misses challenged less with one left (batters 22.9% against 27.6%). Predictions 1 and 3 held, the falsifier did not trigger; the overturn prediction failed (the sign is reversed: teams down to one are the ones that already misjudged), the placebo interval excludes 1 (a shared game factor, which pushes the own estimate toward no effect), the swing and ninth-inning predictions failed. **Reading: running low on challenges makes a team hold back on the challenge itself, not at the plate, so more of the calls that went against it stand: batting, the zone widens on its hitters (the practitioners' point); fielding, it shrinks on its pitchers, by more; and the remaining challenges are not better aimed.** | research |

| PRESSURE-01 | Do hitters decide differently under pressure? Hitter maps pool every game state; if hitters press or tighten with runners in scoring position or late in close games, their swings at the same apparent spots differ from the map, and a postseason aim plan should know it. MATCHUP-05F's representation fitted on 2023-2024; every 2025 pitch to a hitter with a map joined to its plate appearance's state (plate-appearance history: outs, runners, inning, score); his swing with the full map's prediction as offset, on runners in scoring position, late and close (seventh inning on, within one run), two outs, and the own part's product with the first two, outside and inside the zone; game-bootstrap intervals. Development data. Checked on synthetic seasons first: a planted +0.30 log-odds chase with runners in scoring position comes back +0.301 [0.276, 0.316]; with nothing planted, -0.004 [-0.025, +0.011] | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: (1) outside the zone hitters chase more with runners in scoring position than their maps say (positive, interval excluding zero); (2) the own part pulls no less under pressure (both products' intervals reach zero or lie above it). Decision rule: descriptive; the scouting view states any state where hitters' chasing departs from their maps by more than a point. Results (run 37905862764; 305,771 outside and 311,856 inside pitches, all matched; runners in scoring position on 23%, late and close 11%): **outside the zone, with runners in scoring position hitters swing +0.193 log-odds [0.167, 0.219] more than their maps say, about +2.6 points of chase; late and close +0.068 [0.026, 0.096], +0.9 points; two outs nothing.** In the zone, scoring position +0.246 [0.222, 0.262] (+3.8 points of swing), late and close nothing. The own part's pull: times scoring position +0.005 [-0.048, +0.046] outside and +0.012 inside; times late and close +0.002 [-0.065, +0.063] outside and -0.126 [-0.170, -0.067] inside. **(1) held; (2) held outside the zone, where the edge is priced, and failed in the zone late in close games.** Reading: hitters get more aggressive everywhere with runners in scoring position (a state their pooled maps miss), and their own chase spots keep their pull. The scouting view says so. | (1) held: +2.6 pts chase with RISP; (2) held outside |

| PLAYER-01 | Starting pitcher projections, scored: the simulator's distributions of each starter's strikeouts, batters faced, hits and walks against his actual line, every start of the 2026 regular season (replay with production settings, starter_lines), against a simple baseline from earlier dates only (strikeout share over the prior 365 days shrunk toward 22% by 150 batters, times the hazard's expected batters faced) | **Registered before the run.** Predictions: mean strikeouts within 0.2 of actual; the distributions too narrow (the middle half of each start's distribution holds fewer than half of actual lines), because the simulator treats each pitcher's rates as known; the simulator beats the baseline in strikeout CRPS. Across starts, actual strikeouts on the simulated mean slope 1.10 (1.04 to 1.17): the top fifth projects 6.05 and gets 6.32. Given the batters actually faced, the strikeout rate is 3.6% low (0.211 against 0.219) and only slightly compressed (elasticity 1.04, SE 0.035). Batters faced 21.65 against 21.42: the weakest strikeout fifth goes 0.6 batters shorter than projected. Hits (slope 1.01) and walks (0.99) are calibrated. Team totals (run 37782975068): the whole staff is short, not only starters: strikeouts per batter 0.2107 simulated against 0.2214 actual (starters 3.8%, bullpens 6.6% short), walks 3.4% short, hits 1.1% over, and 0.83 more batters per team-game than real games. The model's own residuals on real plate appearances after the context offsets (research run 37783964545) show no such strikeout gap (starters 0.987, relievers 1.024 observed over predicted in 2026), so the gap arises in the simulation; a check of the full probability stack on every real plate appearance is running. | -0.053 CRPS vs baseline (K) |

That explains the starters' strikeout shortfall in the simulated lines (1.038) entirely and most of the bullpens' (1.066; the rest is which relievers pitch). The research lane's model with the same context offsets is close on strikeouts (0.987 starters, 1.024 relievers), so part of the gap is between how features are built for training and how the simulator builds them, and the context offsets were estimated for the earlier locked model. **Registered before the validation replay:** with prior-date role corrections, simulated strikeouts and walks per batter come within 1.5% of actual (bullpens may stay about 1.5% short); starter strikeout projections improve (mean within 0.05, lower CRPS); mean total runs may drop 0.1 to 0.15 and would then need re-centering; win chances within noise. Predictions held for the starters, the walks and the win chances; the bullpen level and the run drop were off. **Not adopted: the role offsets were patching the physics-table bug found in SKEW-01; the role pattern is re-measured on the fixed stack.** | none (superseded) |
| SKEW-01 | Where the strikeout gap between the fit and the simulator comes from. The model's own 2026 test (fit path) has strikeouts 1.007 observed over predicted; the same model in the simulator, on the same plate appearances, about 1.04. Candidate: the simulator's pitch-physics table holds the previous and the current season (as the live runtime loads it), while the fit built every physics feature from all seasons since 2023 and only regular-season rows, so two count features (pitches seen by the pitcher, balls in play by the batter) and every shrunk rate differ between fit and use. Test: the real plate appearance check with each layer's sums recorded (bare model, context, environment, team), once with the live physics seasons (run 37795589978) and once with every season since 2023 joined to the plate-appearance history as the fit does (run 37795641233) | **Registered at 14:50 UTC, October 8 (commit 7734a249), a minute after both runs were queued and before either produced output.** Predictions: with live physics the bare model is about 3 to 4.5% short of strikeouts (observed over predicted 1.03 to 1.045) and about 1.5% over on balls-in-play outs; with fitted physics strikeouts come within 1.5% (1.00 to 1.015), close to the fit's own 1.007; the context, environment and team layers each move the strikeout level by under 1%. If the fitted physics leaves strikeouts at 1.03 or more, this candidate is wrong and the next are the age and rest features. Results (183,685 plate appearances, both runs): **confirmed.** With every season since 2023 joined to the history (733,027 rows, exactly the fit's own count), the bare model reproduces the fit's 2026 test level class by class (observed over predicted: strikeouts 1.007, walks 1.049, singles 1.005, doubles and triples 0.948, home runs 0.962, balls-in-play outs 0.991; the fit's test 1.007, 1.049, 1.006, 0.947, 0.961, 0.991). With the live seasons the bare model is 4.9% short of strikeouts (1.049, a little beyond the 3 to 4.5% predicted), 2.8% over on balls-in-play outs (1.5% predicted) and over on power (doubles and triples 0.923, home runs 0.924): with only two seasons of physics, established pitchers look less established and hitters stronger. Context offsets move strikeouts by 0.6%, environment and team offsets by under 0.1% (as predicted). The full stack with fitted physics: strikeouts 1.001, walks 1.027, doubles and triples 0.990, home runs 0.991, balls-in-play outs 0.995 (production now: 1.043, 1.041, 0.965, 0.952, 0.976). A role pattern remains (strikeouts: starters 0.986, relievers 1.021), the size the research lane showed. **Fix built (brl_live/physics_inputs.SEASONS 'fit'); at the game level it made totals worse in both seasons and stays off (SKEW-02).** | diagnosis |
| SKEW-02 | The physics fix (SKEW-01) at the game level: full replays with production settings, every physics season since 2023 joined to the history, 1,000 worlds, same seeds as CENTER-01 (2026 run 37795885542, 2025 run 37795937194) | **Registered at 15:49 UTC, October 8 (commit b535eb20), before either replay finished.** Predictions: simulated strikeouts per batter (whole staff) within 1.5% of actual in both seasons (2026 now 4.9% short, 2025 4.0%); starters' projected strikeouts within 0.1 of actual with a lower CRPS than 1.2357 (2026); mean total runs fall 0.1 to 0.3 (fewer home runs and extra-base hits, more strikeouts) and land below actual; win Brier within noise, more likely better than worse; correlation with the market's closing log-odds not lower. Decision rule: the fix goes live unless win chances get clearly worse (interval above zero in either season), because the model's tested calibration only holds with the inputs it was fitted on; a run level that lands low is then a separate fix. Results: 2026 (2,454 games): strikeouts per batter 1.009 observed over simulated (was 1.051), starters 0.987; win Brier +0.00052 [-0.00063, +0.00165]; mean total 8.88 against 8.96 before and 8.95 actual; total runs squared error +0.107 [+0.018, +0.205]; P(over the line) Brier +0.00117 [+0.00016, +0.00220]; correlation with the market's total line 0.769 to 0.756; against the closing moneyline the simulator's log-odds spread widens (SD 0.321 to 0.360) and their correlation with the market's drops (0.866 to 0.863); starter strikeouts 4.81 projected against 4.70 actual, CRPS 1.2452 (worse than 1.2357). 2025 (2,423 games): strikeouts 1.017 (was 1.040), starters 1.001; win Brier +0.00011 [-0.00061, +0.00080]; mean total 8.92 against 8.89 before and 8.90 actual; squared error +0.055 [-0.005, +0.116]; P(over) Brier +0.00095 [+0.00007, +0.00186]; spread 0.333 to 0.354. Plate-appearance log loss of the full stack, same plate appearances (check-only runs): fitted physics minus live -0.00005 [-0.00018, +0.00008] (2025) and +0.00003 [-0.00018, +0.00023] (2026); the bare model alone is better with fitted physics (-0.00018 and -0.00017), but the context offsets already absorb the level gap. Predictions: strikeout level held in 2026, missed in 2025 (1.7%); the run drop was wrong (-0.08 and +0.03); win chances within noise but worse in both, not better; market correlation lower, not higher. **Not switched, overriding the registered rule** (which would have switched because the win-chance intervals include zero): its premise was that calibration only holds with the fitted inputs, but at the full stack the plate-appearance log loss is equal and the totals got worse in both seasons with P(over) intervals above zero. Reading: two seasons of physics give more current and more shrunk player estimates; with every season since 2023 the simulator is more spread out than the market and the results support. The consistent fix is to fit and serve the same recent window (a retrain), not to serve more history to the current fit. | none (rejected) |

| BULLPEN-01 | Which reliever enters. The simulator's hand-set reliever scoring (role from the last 365 days, leverage, platoon) against every real 2026 relief entry: it picks the actual reliever 18% of the time, and its picks have a lower prior strikeout rate than real managers' (.2375 against .2405), part of the bullpens' strikeout shortfall in the replays (observed over simulated 1.036 for bullpens against 1.006 for starters in 2026, 1.012 against 0.991 in 2025). Practitioner input pointed at the same inputs: bullpen order, who pitched yesterday and back to back, the next hitters' hands. Built: a conditional logit over each entry's candidates (the replay's bullpen, relievers the team used in the 14 days before, minus those already in the game) on recent use (share of the team's relief batters over 14 and 30 days, pitched yesterday, back to back, appearances over three days, days since the last outing), role over 365 days and over the last 21 days (ninth-inning and eighth-or-later shares, median batters per outing), strikeout record against the other candidates (shrunk), the platoon with the next batter and with the next three, and the situation (save, tied late, close late, early, blowout, mid-inning, extras); 63,415 entries 2023-2026, 4,731 with the reliever outside the set left out (tools/brl_reliever_choice.py; research_lab.game_sim.reliever_choice serves the fit and the simulator with the same feature code). Out of sample: 2026 (fit 2023-2025) top-1 32.9%, log-likelihood -1.765 per entry (uniform -2.200); 2025 (fit 2023, 2024, 2026) 33.8%, -1.733 (uniform -2.174). Largest terms: ninth-inning share in save and tied-late spots (+1.8 and +2.6), the last three weeks' ninth-inning share in saves (+1.9 more), pitched yesterday (-1.3) and back to back (-1.6 more), each of the next three hitters batting from the reliever's side (+0.67, mostly before the eighth). Off, the engine draws exactly the same games as before (checked against the previous commit on 60 seeded games). Test: full replays with the CTX-02 replays' settings plus the choice (2026 with the 2023-2025 fit, 2025 with the 2023, 2024, 2026 fit), same seeds | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before any replay.** Predictions against the CTX-02 replays: simulated bullpen strikeouts per batter up 0.5% to 2.5% in both seasons (observed over simulated from 1.036 to 1.011-1.031 in 2026, from 1.012 to 0.987-1.007 in 2025); bullpen hits per batter down 0.3% to 1.5%; starters unchanged (batters faced within 0.05, strikeouts per batter within 0.2%); mean total down 0.00 to 0.06 runs; total runs squared error within 0.03 either way; win Brier not worse beyond noise (the paired interval's upper end below +0.0003) in both seasons. Decision rule: on if the bullpen strikeout rate moves toward the actual one in both seasons, the win Brier is not worse beyond noise in either and the squared error is not worse by more than 0.03 in either; then the 2023-2026 table (brl_live/reliever_choice.json, ADJUST reliever_choice) goes on for pregame and in-game runs. Results (runs 37851994243, 37852034410; 1,000 worlds, same seeds): bullpen strikeouts per batter 0.2166 to 0.2220 (observed over simulated 1.036 to 1.010, 2026) and 0.2249 to 0.2305 (1.012 to 0.988, 2025), +2.5% in both; bullpen hits per batter -1.0% and -1.2%; starters unchanged (21.64 and 22.00 batters, strikeouts per batter within 0.0001); mean total 8.98 to 8.92 (actual 8.95) and 8.92 to 8.85 (8.90), -0.07 in both; total runs squared error -0.002 and +0.011; P(over the line) Brier -0.00017 and -0.00078 [-0.00164, +0.00009]; simulator win Brier -0.00038 [-0.00126, +0.00047] (2026) and -0.00040 [-0.00116, +0.00037] (2025). Predictions held for the bullpen rates (2026 at the edge of the range: 1.010 against 1.011), starters and squared error; the totals fell slightly more than predicted (-0.07 against at most -0.06); the win Brier is better in both seasons, but the registered noise bound (the interval's upper end below +0.0003) is not met in either. | RUNS-01 | Where the simulator's run level comes from. With real outcomes the engine converts too few runs without the running plays (-0.07 per team per nine innings in 2026, TRANS-02's check), yet the replays run at the actual level (8.98 against 8.95), so the simulated outcomes carry about +0.08 runs per team-game more than real ones would; the team rates explain about +0.03 (2026 replay with the production context table: hits per batter 1% high, walks 2% low, strikeouts 1.4% low). The PA model has bases, outs and score among its features; the context offsets are by side and inning only. Check: the full probability stack on every real plate appearance by bases (empty, first only, a runner in scoring position) and outs, 2026 with the 2025 context table and 2025 with the 2026 table (check-only replays) | **Registered before the run.** Reading set now: if hits are over-predicted with runners on more than with the bases empty (observed over predicted for hits at least 2% lower with a runner in scoring position than with the bases empty, in both seasons), the excess is a calibration by base state, and a base-state offset table estimated like CTX-02 is the fix to replay; if the groups agree within 2%, the excess is in who bats and pitches (bullpen choice, starters' length) or in the extra-base mix, checked next. Results (runs 37852951760, 37852989061; every real plate appearance, 183,685 in 2026 and 182,487 in 2025): hits observed over predicted 0.981, 1.000 and 0.987 (bases empty, first only, scoring position) in 2026 and 1.010, 1.009, 1.014 in 2025, within 2%, so the reading set in advance points away from calibration by base state; the stack's season-level miss is worth +0.038 (2026) and +0.008 (2025) runs per team-game by linear weights; the shape by bases and outs is consistent across seasons and is taken up in RUNS-02. | measurement |
| LEASH-01 | How long a start runs, beyond the starter's record. The fitted hazard pulls a starter against his shrunk average batters faced over every earlier start, which knows nothing about the start in front of it. Simulated against actual batters faced, every start of the CTX-02 replays (9,700 starts; facts from earlier appearances only): openers and starters on four days' rest or fewer +8.2 batters too long (sim 16.4, actual 8.3), starts after a relief outing +4.7, the first start back from a layoff of 20 days or more +1.5 and the second +0.7, March +1.1 and September +1.0, while regular starts on normal rest run 0.3 to 0.4 short (the average, +0.14, hides both). Practitioner input pointed the same way (layoffs, IL returns and the last outing drive how long a starter goes). Built: research_lab.game_sim.starter_leash: a shift in batters faced per start from these facts, regressed on the simulated-minus-actual starts of another season's replay (tools/brl_leash.py; coefficients stable across seasons: short rest -7.4 and -6.5, relief before -1.5 and -2.1, first back -1.8 and -2.1, second back -0.9 and -0.8, March +0.4 and +0.6, September -0.9 and -0.9, regular +0.5 and +0.6), delivered through the hazard's expected batters along its measured response (league-average hitting: expected 12, 16, 19, 22, 25 give 5.1, 11.8, 17.8, 21.6, 24.4 batters); regular season only (the postseason keeps its measured x0.91). Test: full replays with the CTX-02 replays' settings plus the table from the other season (2026 with the 2025 table, 2025 with the 2026 table), same seeds; starter lines scored as in PLAYER-01 | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before any replay.** Predictions: starters' simulated minus actual batters faced within 0.10 in both seasons (now +0.21 and +0.08); the root mean squared error of each start's simulated mean down by 0.2 to 0.5 (now 4.41 and 4.04); short rest within 2.0 (now +8.0, +8.5), relief before within 1.5 (+4.9, +4.5), first back within 0.6, regular starts within 0.25; batters-faced CRPS better by at least 0.05 (2.274, 2.104) and strikeout CRPS better by at least 0.005 (1.235, 1.245) in both seasons; win Brier not worse beyond noise (upper end below +0.0003); mean total within 0.04 and total squared error within 0.03 of the reference. Decision rule: on for regular-season games (ADJUST leash, brl_live/leash.json from both seasons) if the starters' bias and both CRPS improve in both seasons and neither win Brier nor squared error is worse beyond those bounds. Results (runs 37854594794, 37854634286; 1,000 worlds, same seeds): starters' simulated minus actual batters faced +0.21 to +0.02 (2026) and +0.08 to +0.07 (2025); RMSE of each start's simulated mean 4.41 to 3.87 and 4.04 to 3.68; short rest +8.0 to +0.8 and +8.5 to +1.1; a relief outing before +4.9 to +0.7 and +4.5 to +0.0; first back from a layoff +1.7 to +0.3 and +1.4 to -0.4; regular starts -0.67 to +0.01 and within +0.11 (2025); batters-faced CRPS 2.274 to 2.046 and 2.104 to 1.958, correlation of the simulated mean with actual 0.53 to 0.67 and 0.41 to 0.56; strikeout CRPS 1.235 to 1.209 and 1.245 to 1.230; hits and walks CRPS better in both; mean total unchanged (8.984, 8.923); total runs squared error +0.008 and +0.003; simulator win Brier +0.00031 [-0.00012, +0.00072] (2026) and -0.00012 [-0.00050, +0.00025] (2025), pooled +0.00009 [-0.00019, +0.00038]. Every starter prediction held (the 2026 RMSE fell 0.54, past the 0.5 predicted); the 2026 win Brier bound (upper end below +0.0003) failed. **Not switched on under the registered rule.** The starter lines on the page are much better with it and the win chance is unchanged pooled, so it is retested inside the full package with the fitted bullpen (more innings move to bullpens, which were the weakest part of the simulator). | starter K CRPS -0.026 (2026), -0.015 (2025); not adopted yet |
| RUNS-02 | The stack's shape by situation. The RUNS-01 check runs (2026 with the 2025 context table, run 37852951760; 2025 with the 2026 table, run 37852989061) show the probability stack's miss by bases and outs, relative to its miss over each season, the same in both seasons: walks with a runner on first only 9 to 10% more than predicted with none out and 7 to 10% with one out; walks with the bases empty and one or two out 4 to 7% fewer; strikeouts with a runner in scoring position and none out 7% fewer; singles with runners on and one out 4 to 5% more; home runs with a runner on first and none out 7 to 11% fewer, leadoff home runs 3 to 6% more; reaching base on errors and the like up to a third more with runners on. Hits agree within 2% across base groups (0.981, 1.000, 0.987 in 2026; 1.010, 1.009, 1.014 in 2025), so RUNS-01's base-state reading is not met; the season level moves the other way between seasons (2026 with the 2025 table: extra-base hits and home runs 4% over-predicted, walks 5% under; 2025 with the 2026 table the reverse), worth +0.038 and +0.008 runs per team-game by linear weights. Built: brl_live.provider_adjust.BaseStateAdjust, nine cells (bases empty, first only, a runner in scoring position, by outs), log(cell ratio / season ratio) shrunk toward the base group by 2,000 plate appearances (tools/brl_base_state.py), after the team offsets. In the engine with league-average hitting the shape adds runs: +0.052 per game (40,000 paired games, SE 0.015), so the simulator's excess over its parts lies elsewhere (next: who bats; the simulator keeps the nine starters all game). Test: full replays with the CTX-02 replays' settings plus the other season's shape, same seeds | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before any replay.** Predictions against the CTX-02 replays: mean total up 0.02 to 0.08 in both seasons; walks per batter up 0.5% to 2%; win Brier within 0.0004 either way; total runs squared error within 0.03 and P(over) Brier within 0.0005 either way. Decision rule: the shape is a calibration fix, but it moves the level the wrong way, so it goes on only together with a fix for the level, unless the win Brier improves in both seasons. Results (runs 37856958529, 37856998314): mean total 8.98 to 9.05 (actual 8.96, 2026) and 8.92 to 8.98 (8.90, 2025); walks per batter unchanged in both (0.0964, 0.0995), the shape moving walks between situations without changing their number; win Brier +0.00007 [-0.00046, +0.00064] (2026) and -0.00045 [-0.00095, +0.00003] (2025), pooled -0.00019 [-0.00054, +0.00018]; total runs squared error +0.020 and +0.036; P(over) Brier +0.0003 and +0.0004. Predictions held for the run level, the win Brier and P(over); failed for walks (no change) and narrowly for the 2025 squared error (+0.036 against 0.03). **Not on alone under the rule** (the win Brier improved in 2025 only); it is part of PROD-02's P2. | -0.00019 pooled, not adopted alone |
| RELIEF-02 | When relievers come out. The engine's hand-set rule for relievers against every real relief decision point 2023-2026 (after each plate appearance a reliever completes in a game that goes on; local plate-appearance history, aggregates only): in the middle of an inning, where a change is allowed (three batters faced or more), real managers make a change at 9% of decision points and the rule at 56% to 57%; at an inning's end 56% to 60% real, 61% the rule. In the engine (league-average hitting, fitted starter hazard) that means 4.6 relievers a team-game for 3.5 batters each, against 3.3 and 4.9 in real 2025-2026 games: the simulator reached deep into its bullpens (in the replays every reliever used in the last 14 days is a candidate), part of why simulated bullpens struck out fewer and allowed more hits than real ones. Built: research_lab.game_sim.relief_exit.ReliefExit, logistic hazards for the middle of an inning and for an inning's end on batters faced, runs charged (runs scored while he pitched minus runners inherited), his median relief length over a year, his ninth- and eighth-inning role, the inning, the lead and, mid-inning, runners and outs (tools/brl_relief_exit.py; the same feature code serves the fit and the engine). Out of sample, log-likelihood per decision: 2026 (fit 2023-2025) mid-inning -0.265 against -1.780 for the hand-set rule, inning's end -0.533 against -0.883; 2025 (fit 2023, 2024, 2026) -0.285 against -1.772 and -0.538 against -0.851; removal rates 9.3% fitted against 8.8% and 9.6% real mid-inning. In the engine: 3.4 relievers a team-game for 4.8 batters each (real 3.3 and 4.9). Tested together with the reliever choice in BULLPEN-02. | measurement and build | model fit |
| BULLPEN-02 | The bullpen as a whole: the fitted reliever choice (BULLPEN-01) and the fitted exits (RELIEF-02) together, full replays with the CTX-02 replays' settings plus both (2026 with the 2023-2025 fits, 2025 with the 2023, 2024, 2026 fits), same seeds; the replay rows now record the pitchers each team uses | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before any replay.** Predictions against the CTX-02 replays: pitchers per team-game within 0.3 of actual in both seasons; bullpen strikeouts per batter within 1.5% of actual in both (now 3.6% and 1.2% short); bullpen hits per batter down 0.5% to 2.5%; starters unchanged (batters faced within 0.1); mean total down 0.00 to 0.12; win Brier better in each season and, pooled over both seasons' paired differences by date, the upper end of the 95% interval below +0.0003; total runs squared error within 0.03 either way; P(over) Brier not worse by more than 0.0005. Decision rule: on (ADJUST reliever_choice and relief_exit, the 2023-2026 tables) if the pooled win Brier bound holds, neither season's squared error is worse by more than 0.03, and the bullpen strikeout rate is within 1.5% of actual in both seasons. Results (runs 37857987864, 37858023424; 1,000 worlds, same seeds): pitchers per team-game 4.17 simulated against 4.28 actual (2026) and 4.19 against 4.29 (2025), the hand-set rule gave about 5.6 in the engine; across teams the simulated count follows the actual one at 0.54 and 0.43 (SD across teams 0.12 against 0.15); bullpen strikeouts per batter observed over simulated 1.036 to 1.015 and 1.012 to 0.992; bullpen hits per batter -0.8% and -0.9%; starters unchanged; mean total 8.98 to 8.93 (actual 8.96) and 8.92 to 8.87 (8.90); total runs squared error +0.019 and +0.029; P(over) Brier -0.0002 in both; win Brier -0.00020 [-0.00108, +0.00065] (2026) and -0.00032 [-0.00105, +0.00042] (2025), pooled -0.00026 [-0.00085, +0.000304]. Every prediction held except the pooled bound: its upper end is +0.000304 against +0.0003. **Not switched on under the registered rule** (missed by 0.000004; no re-run of the bootstrap to get under it). The exits made the bullpens real (counts, strikeouts) without moving win chances beyond the choice alone (pooled -0.00040 for BULLPEN-01); the package test PROD-02 decides. | -0.00026 pooled, not adopted alone |
| RELIEF-03 | Teams' bullpen style. Against the fitted exit hazard (RELIEF-02), team-season residuals of reliever removals are large and stable: SD 2.7 points mid-inning on a 9.2% base rate and 5.2 points at an inning's end on 58%; odd against even games 0.76 and 0.79; season to season 0.65, 0.61, 0.31 and 0.66, 0.66, 0.28 (2025 to 2026 lower, managers change). Some teams pull relievers at an inning's end far more than the hazard expects (one-inning roles: Philadelphia 2024-2025 +12 to +15 points), others keep them for more innings (Texas 2023-2025 -11 to -13). Built: brl_replay.relief_decisions.HookOffsets, per team and decision type the decayed sum of observed minus fitted removals over the decayed information p(1 - p) plus k, prior dates only; the offset moves the hazard's log-odds (ReliefExit.remove, ManagerPolicy.relief_offsets); production table brl_live/relief_hooks.json (tools/brl_relief_hooks.py). Tuned out of sample on each season with the hazard fitted on the others: half-life 90 days and k 10 best in both, +0.0020 (2026) and +0.0037 (2025) log-likelihood per decision. Test: BULLPEN-02's replays plus the offsets, same seeds | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before any replay.** Predictions against BULLPEN-02: the correlation across teams between simulated and actual relievers per game up by at least 0.10 in both seasons; pooled win Brier within 0.0002 either way; mean total within 0.03. Decision rule: on together with BULLPEN-02 (ADJUST relief_hooks) if the usage correlation rises in both seasons and the pooled win Brier difference is not above +0.0001. Results (runs 37860073667, 37860108046; 1,000 worlds, same seeds): across teams the simulated pitchers per game follow the actual count at 0.70 (2026, from 0.54) and 0.56 (2025, from 0.43), SD across teams 0.148 and 0.134 against actual 0.150 and 0.153 (both 0.117 before); pitchers per team-game 4.14 against 4.28 (2026) and 4.21 against 4.29 (2025); mean total 8.93 and 8.87, unchanged; total runs squared error -0.006 and -0.003; win Brier against BULLPEN-02 -0.00019 [-0.00050, +0.00013] (2026) and +0.000003 [-0.00033, +0.00034] (2025), pooled -0.00009 [-0.00032, +0.00014]; against the CTX-02 replays pooled -0.00036 [-0.00092, +0.00021]. Every prediction held. **Passes its rule: on together with BULLPEN-02**; BULLPEN-02 did not pass alone, so the package test PROD-02 decides whether either goes live. | -0.00009 pooled vs BULLPEN-02, on with the package |
| POST-02 | Postseason reliever exits. Postseason managers use 4.2 relievers a team-game against 3.3 in the regular season (POST-01). In the engine (league-average hitting, fitted starter hazard with the postseason x0.91, ten-man bullpen, 1,200 games): the hand-set rule gives 5.28 relievers a team-game, the fitted exits 3.77, and the fitted exits with +0.4 on the hazard's log-odds 4.15. Built: ADJUST postseason_exit_offset (Wild Card, Division and League series +0.4; World Series 0, as for starters), added to the team hook offsets in brl_live.boxscore.manager_for; it acts only when the fitted exits are on. | Calibrated on realism, as POST-01 was (postseason games are too few to replay); goes on with the fitted exits if BULLPEN-02 passes. | realism |
| PROD-02 | The simulator with every structural fix of October 8 together, against the CTX-02 replays, same seeds: P1 = the fitted reliever choice and exits with team hooks (BULLPEN-01, RELIEF-02, RELIEF-03), the starter leash (LEASH-01) and the running plays between plate appearances (TRANS-02); P2 = P1 plus the shape by bases and outs (RUNS-02). Each was built against a measured miss; alone, the choice and the plays made win chances better and the leash fixed starter lines, while the run level moved in both directions (choice -0.07, plays +0.14, shape +0.07). Cross-season tables as in each component's test | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before any replay.** Predictions for P1: pooled win Brier better than the reference by 0.0004 to 0.0015; relievers per team-game within 0.3 of actual and starters' batters faced within 0.1 in both seasons; mean total within 0.12 of actual in both. For P2: mean total 0.04 to 0.09 above P1, win Brier within 0.0003 of P1. Decision rule (on for pregame and in-game runs, ADJUST reliever_choice, relief_exit, relief_hooks, leash, running_events, and base_state for P2): the pooled win Brier difference against the reference has its upper end below +0.0003 and its point estimate is not above +0.0002 in either season; each season's total runs squared error is not worse by more than 0.05 and its mean total is within 0.12 of actual; P2 is chosen over P1 if its pooled win Brier is no worse than P1's and its squared error is not worse than P1's by more than 0.02 in either season. Results, P1 (runs 37863329907, 37863360862; 1,000 worlds, same seeds): win Brier -0.00004 [-0.00109, +0.00104] (2026) and -0.00062 [-0.00155, +0.00033] (2025), pooled -0.00033 [-0.00101, +0.00034]; total runs squared error +0.063 [-0.012, +0.135] and +0.067 [-0.015, +0.148], pooled +0.065 [+0.009, +0.121]; mean total 9.07 against 8.96 actual (reference 8.98) and 9.01 against 8.90 (8.92); P(over the line) Brier +0.0004 and +0.0012 [+0.0001, +0.0022]; pitchers per team-game 4.16 against 4.28 and 4.20 against 4.29, across teams 0.79 and 0.63; starters' batters faced 21.44 against 21.42 and 21.99 against 21.92. Predictions held for relievers, starters and the run level; the win Brier gain (-0.00033) fell short of the predicted 0.0004 to 0.0015. **P1 fails its rule**: the squared error in both seasons and the pooled win bound (+0.00034 against +0.0003). The totals miss is the running plays' (TRANS-02 alone: +0.063 and +0.075). P2 adds the shape, which adds runs (RUNS-02: squared error +0.020 and +0.036), so it cannot pass the same criterion; its 2025 replay is left to finish (the runners' token cannot cancel runs) and is recorded for the shape's effect only. P2 (runs 37863398520, 37863432413): against P1 win Brier -0.00034 and +0.00014, pooled -0.00010 [-0.00045, +0.00028], squared error +0.008 and +0.021, mean total +0.07 in both (9.14 and 9.06 against 8.96 and 8.90 actual); against the reference pooled win -0.00043 [-0.00110, +0.00026] and squared error +0.080 [+0.015, +0.142]. P2 fails on totals as well. Nothing switched on; PROD-03 tests the package without the plays. | P1 and P2 fail (totals); not adopted |
| PROD-03 | Bullpens and starters run the way real managers run them, without the running plays: the fitted reliever choice and exits with team hooks (BULLPEN-01, RELIEF-02, RELIEF-03) and the starter leash (LEASH-01), against the CTX-02 replays, same seeds, the same cross-season tables as PROD-02's P1. P1 (the same plus the running plays) failed on totals in both seasons (squared error +0.063 and +0.067, pooled +0.065 [+0.009, +0.121]), the running plays' own miss in TRANS-02, while its win Brier went the right way (pooled -0.00033 [-0.00101, +0.00034]). Each of these fixes was fitted to real manager decisions or real starts, never to game results; this test asks whether together they cost any accuracy | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before any replay.** Predictions against the CTX-02 replays: pooled win Brier better by 0.0001 to 0.0006; pitchers per team-game within 0.2 of actual and their correlation across teams at least 0.55 in both seasons; starters' batters faced within 0.1 of actual in both; mean total 0.02 to 0.08 below the reference in both; pooled total runs squared error between -0.01 and +0.04; P(over the line) Brier within 0.0006 of the reference in each season. Decision rule, set as a do-no-harm test because the fixes are justified by the structure they repair (PROD-02's bound, the pooled interval's upper end below +0.0003, needs a gain near 0.0004 to pass, more than structural fixes can be expected to show in two seasons of games): on for pregame and in-game runs (ADJUST reliever_choice, relief_exit, relief_hooks and leash; the postseason exit offset acts with the exits) if the pooled win Brier difference is not above zero and neither season's is above +0.0002; the pooled total runs squared error difference is not above +0.03 and neither season's is above +0.06; the games-weighted P(over) difference is not above +0.0003; the mean total is within 0.10 of actual in both seasons; and the reliever and starter predictions hold. Results (runs 37873004376 and 37873038539, same seeds as CTX-02; 2,427 and 2,423 games): win Brier -0.00024 (2026) and -0.00039 (2025), pooled -0.00031 [-0.00088, +0.00025]; total runs squared error +0.009 and +0.042, pooled +0.025 [-0.016, +0.067]; P(over the line) -0.00035 and -0.00007, pooled -0.00023 [-0.00074, +0.00028]; mean total 8.93 against 8.96 actual (reference 8.98) and 8.87 against 8.90 (reference 8.92), 0.05 and 0.06 below the reference; pitchers per team-game 4.16 against 4.28 actual and 4.21 against 4.29, correlation across teams 0.79 and 0.63; starters' batters faced 21.44 against 21.42 and 21.99 against 21.92. Every prediction held and every condition of the do-no-harm rule is met (the pooled squared-error bound +0.03 with 0.005 to spare). The market-taught headline refitted on these replays moves little (simulator weight 0.543 to 0.561, team model 0.545 to 0.524) and its cross-season checks improve slightly (2026 against the closing line -0.00032 against -0.00027 with the reference replays; 2025 -0.00089 against -0.00083). **Passed; switched on in the live settings on October 9, 2026, at about 8 a.m. Eastern with Alec's approval.** Gate passed: the registered do-no-harm rule on full replays of 2025 and 2026. The win Brier gain is a point estimate whose pooled interval includes zero, so the gain itself is a product call (the realism measures, pitchers per game and starters' batters faced, are the clear improvement). The headline version taught-v5 (intercept 0.0395, simulator 0.561, team model 0.524) is in force for every game whose first pitch is after 12:30 UTC October 9. | passed; live from October 9 |
| ZONE-02 | The 2026 plate reference, measured directly (ZONE-01 was confounded): the same pitches matched between the feed and the Savant export (game, at-bat, pitch number; 2025 and 2026 through July), the feed's height against Savant's reported plate_z and against Savant's flight evaluated at the front and at the middle of the plate; Savant's own reference from each flight run back to its release point | Measurement. Decision rule set before the run: if the feed's 2026 heights match Savant's reported plate_z and the release check puts Savant's 2026 reference at the middle of the plate, every model that pools 2026 feed locations with earlier seasons evaluates 2026 pitches at the front of the plate from their flights (and the MATCHUP-01 untouched scoring does the same); otherwise feed locations are used as reported. Results (run 37878183381; 708,985 pitches matched in 2025, 484,833 in 2026): 2025 feed minus Savant plate_z +0.003 in, against the front-of-plate evaluation +0.003, the middle +0.949; 2026 feed minus Savant plate_z +0.939 in (90th percentile of the absolute gap 1.36), feed minus the front-of-plate evaluation +0.003, the middle +0.939. Savant's own reference by the release check: front in 2025 (offset -0.003 in, SD 0.043), middle in 2026 (-0.003, SD 0.043). **The feed did not change: its 2026 heights are at the front of the plate, like every earlier season; only Savant's export moved to the middle.** Under the rule, feed locations are used as reported: pooling 2023-2026 feed pitches stands, the MATCHUP-01 untouched scoring runs without the 2026 shift, and ZONE-01's failed prediction was the right answer (its confound was the 2026 called-strike change, not coordinates). Savant-based work keeps evaluating each flight at the front of the plate (tools/brl_savant.anchor). | feed unchanged (front of plate); no harmonizing |
| DATA-02 | Savant pitch-by-pitch data for the matchup program (discovery/MATCHUP_PROGRAM.md): every regular-season pitch of 2024 to 2026 from the public search export, one day at a time, inside Actions (experiment savant_pull): fitted flight (vx0 to az at 50 ft), plate location, ABS zone, arm angle, run value, batted ball and spray, and bat tracking on swings (bat speed, swing length, attack angle and direction, swing path tilt, intercept point). Sealed with the package key, private/savant/pitches-YEAR-PART.enc on the ledger branch; the receipt carries coverage counts and league summaries only. The 2026 postseason is not pulled yet (untouched set) | Data acquisition, no hypothesis. The receipt also settles which plate reference the export uses each season: anchoring each flight on the reported crossing, front or middle of the plate, and running it back to the reported release point (the right reference leaves the same offset every season). Results (run 37875412419): 2025 and 2026 regular seasons pulled, sealed and stored (private/savant/pitches-2025-a and -b, 2026-a and -b, 18 to 20 MB each); the 2024 pull was refused by the server (HTTP 403) after about 22 minutes, which ended the job before its receipt summaries were written. Coverage and the plate-reference check are reported by the next job that reads the stored files (CONTACT-01). 2024 is retried later with fewer parallel requests. Coverage (CONTACT-01 run 37877565820): 712,528 pitches in 2025 and 487,063 in 2026 through July; bat tracking filled on 96 to 98% of swings every month, whiffs included (attack angle on 98.7% of whiffs, contact point on 98.3%); league attack angle 9.1 degrees (SD 11.9), swing path tilt 32.2, bat speed 69.6 mph; arm angle on 99%. **Plate reference, settled per pitch**: running each flight back to its release point, the front-of-plate reference leaves no offset in 2025 (-0.003 in, SD 0.04) and the middle-of-plate one 0.94 in; in 2026 exactly the reverse (middle -0.003, front -0.94). Savant's plate_x and plate_z are measured at the front of the plate through 2025 and at the middle from 2026, as documented; the average pitch drops 0.94 inches between the two. | 2025 and 2026 stored; reference confirmed |
| ZONE-01 | Pitch location across the 2026 definition change. Statcast documents that plate_x and plate_z moved from the front of the plate (through 2025) to the middle (2026) and that sz_top and sz_bot became the ABS zone. Our discovery work trains location models on 2023-2025 and tests on 2026 locations (Decision Horizon), and the player model's zone, chase and in-zone contact rates read the Statcast zone field across seasons. Test (discovery lane, experiment zone_audit, 2023-2026 feed pitches, aggregates only): for the same pitcher and pitch type in consecutive seasons (at least 50 pitches each), the change in mean height at the plate against the drop each pitch makes between the front and the middle of the plate, computed from its own rebuilt flight in a third season (a change of definition scales with how steeply the pitch arrives; a change in where pitchers aim does not); the zone field's in-zone share and called strikes by zone and height, by season | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: 2025 to 2026, the slope of the change on the computed drop between 0.7 and 1.3 with its interval excluding zero, the mean change between -0.5 and -1.2 inches, curveballs dropping at least 0.4 inches more than four-seamers; 2023 to 2024 and 2024 to 2025, slopes within -0.35 and +0.35. Decision rule: if the 2025 to 2026 prediction holds, every location model that pools 2026 with earlier seasons uses front-of-plate heights for 2026 (each pitch's measured height minus its computed drop, and the same for side), and the Decision Horizon results that tested on 2026 locations are rechecked with corrected locations; if not, the documented change is not in the feed's coordinates and pooling stands. Results (run 37874888511, 2,874,000 feed pitches, 1,057 to 1,095 pitcher-type pairs per season step): computed drop between front and middle of the plate four-seam 0.70, sinker 0.87, slider 1.14, curveball 1.44 inches. 2025 to 2026 the same pitcher-type mean height rose 0.15 inches (four-seam -0.10, curveball +0.50), slope on the computed drop -0.84 [-1.31, -0.43]; controls 2023 to 2024 +0.54 [0.00, 0.98] and 2024 to 2025 -0.38 [-0.88, +0.17], mean changes +0.19 and +0.34. **The prediction failed**: no drop of the documented size appears. But the test is confounded in exactly the season it targets: called strikes moved sharply in 2026 (taken pitches over the middle third at 1.4 to 1.6 ft called strikes 25% against 45 to 52% before, at 3.4 to 3.6 ft 33% against 40 to 49%; in zones 1 to 9 93% against 88 to 90%, outside 4.1% against 4.6 to 7.3%), so pitchers had reason to change where they aim, and the year-to-year controls swing by as much as the effect sought. Recorded as not settling the feed's reference; the release-point check in DATA-02 (no dependence on where pitchers aim) decides it. Until then no experiment pools 2026 feed locations with earlier seasons (MATCHUP-01 uses 2023 to 2025). Side result: the 2026 ABS-era zone is tighter at both edges in feed coordinates. | prediction failed; inconclusive by design |
| MATCHUP-01 | Matchup program M2 (discovery/MATCHUP_PROGRAM.md): does each hitter's own swing map, read where the pitch appears to be headed at the decision moment (gravity-only projection 260 ms before the plate, the Decision Horizon result estimated on 2025 and 2026), predict his swings beyond the league map and additive terms for the hitter and the pitcher (each one's earlier swing rate, shrunk)? Feed pitches 2023-2025 only (2026 locations use another reference): league maps (location bands by count, count, pitch group, speed, both propensities) and per-hitter maps (ridge logistic on 22 location bands and two strikes, offset by the league model, at least 300 training pitches), each on the true crossing and on the decision-moment crossing; shrinkage chosen on the second half of 2024 with maps from 2023 and its first half; maps refitted on 2023-2024 and scored on every 2025 decision, paired by game. Checked on synthetic worlds first: with planted hitter-specific maps the hitter maps gain 10.2 nats per 1,000 decisions, with none -0.2 | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions on 2025: decision-moment over true crossing, league maps, 25 to 50 nats per 1,000 decisions; hitter maps over the league map on the decision-moment crossing, 2 to 8 nats per 1,000 with the interval excluding zero; the decision-moment crossing beats the true one for hitter maps too, by at least 20. Decision rule: if hitter maps on the decision-moment crossing gain at least 2 nats per 1,000 with the interval excluding zero, they become the swing part of the pitch-level matchup engine (with whiffs from CONTACT-01 and called strikes), whose plate-appearance predictions are then tested against the simulator's model; below that, hitter-specific swing maps carry nothing a matchup model can use and the engine takes swing decisions from the league map. Results (run 37876160597, every 2025 swing-or-take decision, 87.7% by hitters with a map): log loss per decision, league map 0.50187 on the true crossing and 0.46899 at the decision moment; hitter maps 0.49373 and 0.45721 (shrinkage 10 chosen on held-out 2024 in both). Gains in nats per 1,000 decisions: decision moment over true crossing, league maps +32.9 [31.9, 33.8]; hitter maps over the league map +8.1 [7.8, 8.5] on the true crossing and **+11.8 [11.4, 12.2] at the decision moment**; hitter maps at the decision moment over the standard hitter heat map on the true crossing **+36.5 [35.5, 37.5]**. Matchups: 5,440 hitter-pitcher pairs with at least 10 pitches outside the zone in 2025 (70,618 pitches): the hitter-map model's predicted chase deviation from the additive model (SD 3.3 points across pairs) predicts the observed deviation with slope **0.98 [0.88, 1.09]** (correlation 0.25 against pair outcomes whose spread, 12.6 points, is mostly sampling noise). Every prediction held in direction; the hitter-map gain at the decision moment (11.8) is above the predicted 2 to 8. **Passes its rule: hitter decision-moment maps become the swing part of the matchup engine.** Reading: each hitter's swing decisions are sharper, and more his own, when read at the moment he commits than at the plate (the per-hitter gain is 45% larger there), and what a specific pitcher's arsenal looks like at that moment predicts how often that hitter chases it, calibrated, a year ahead. Before any claim leaves the lab: one scoring on the untouched set (2026 from August 1) once the 2026 location reference is settled (DATA-02). | +36.5 nats/1000 over standard hitter maps; pair slope 0.98 |
| MATCHUP-01F | MATCHUP-01 scored once on the program's untouched set: every 2026 pitch from August 1 (the regular season's last two months and any postseason pitches in the feed data), never fitted, tuned or inspected by any model of the program. The frozen code is matchup_final in tools/brl_discovery.py as of commit 45576a52 (unchanged since; checked line for line): league swing maps on the true crossing and at the decision moment (gravity-only projection 260 ms out), per-hitter ridge maps with shrinkage 10 and at least 300 training pitches, all fitted on 2023-2025; 2026 feed locations used as reported (ZONE-02: the feed stayed at the front of the plate). Pairs: hitter-pitcher pairs with at least 10 pitches outside the zone in the test set | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run; one scoring, a second look needs a new registration.** Predictions on the untouched set: (a) hitter maps over the league map at the decision moment gain at least 2 nats per 1,000 decisions with the interval excluding zero (MATCHUP-01's own threshold); (b) the decision moment beats the true crossing for the league map, interval excluding zero; (c) hitter maps at the decision moment beat the standard hitter heat map on the true crossing, interval excluding zero; (d) the pair chase slope's interval includes 1 and excludes 0. The test set differs from development in ways that work against the claim: maps are seven to nine months old, and 2026 called strikes moved under the challenge system (ZONE-01), which hitters may have answered by changing what they swing at. Decision rule: if (a) and (d) hold, the decision-moment swing map is credited as a forward-tested result (a hitter's swing decisions and his chases against a specific pitcher's arsenal predicted from earlier seasons, calibrated) and can be stated outside the lab with these numbers; if (a) fails, the development result is not credited and the engine's swing part falls back to the league map plus the hitter's rate. Results (run 37881498012, frozen commit 45576a52; 226,679 untouched decisions from August 1, 2026, 81.3% by hitters with a map; maps from 2023-2025): gains in nats per 1,000 decisions: (a) hitter maps over the league map at the decision moment **+11.2 [10.4, 11.9]** (development +11.8); (b) decision moment over true crossing, league map +36.8 [35.1, 38.5] (development +32.9); (c) hitter maps at the decision moment over the standard hitter heat map on the true crossing **+40.2 [38.5, 41.8]** (development +36.5); hitter maps on the true crossing +7.8 [7.1, 8.4]. Pairs: 820 hitter-pitcher pairs with at least 10 pitches outside the zone, chase slope on the predicted pair deviation **0.95 [0.66, 1.19]**. **Every prediction held on data no model had seen, with frozen code: the result is credited.** A hitter's swing decisions, read where each pitch appears to be headed 260 ms before the plate, are predicted from earlier seasons 40 nats per 1,000 decisions better than by the standard heat map of where pitches cross the plate, the gain is larger than in development, and what a specific pitcher's arsenal looks like at that moment predicts that hitter's chases against him, calibrated (slope near 1), in the two months after the models were frozen. | credited on untouched data: +40.2 over standard maps; pair slope 0.95 |
| CONTACT-01 | **Amended before the run (data):** 2024 could not be pulled (DATA-02), so training uses 2025 swings with hitter history accumulated from 2025 (at least 30 earlier contact swings), scoring 2026 through July as registered; the run also reports the stored seasons' coverage and plate-reference check. Matchup program M1, contact tolerance: theta = AA - abs(VAA), F = abs(sin(theta)) times the hitter's contact-depth spread (inches). AA is the hitter's usual attack angle for a pitch at that height and the spread is that of his contact depth (Savant intercept point toward the pitcher), both from earlier swings only, shrunk; VAA is the pitch's approach angle at the front of the plate from its fitted flight. Savant pitches (DATA-02), training on 2024-2025 swings with hitter history, scoring 2026 through July (the program's untouched set is dropped in code). Models of a whiff on a swing (gradient boosting): G0 the pitch (speed, movement, location, approach angles, spin, extension, release height, count, type, platoon, arm angle); G1 plus the hitter's own whiff rate and bat speed; G2 plus his swing geometry (attack angle, contact-depth spread) for the flexible model to use as it can; and the mechanism, G1 plus one coefficient on F fitted on cross-fitted training logits; F from height instead of the pitch's approach angle as the competitor. Checked on synthetic worlds first: planted, geometry adds 0.38 nats per 1,000 swings, the one-parameter F 0.23 (59 percent of it), loose-timing hitters' angle effect 1.8 times tight ones'; null, all near zero | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run (it waits for DATA-02's data).** Predictions on 2026 through July: swing geometry adds to the hitter's rates in the flexible model, at least 0.2 nats per 1,000 swings with the interval excluding zero; the one-parameter F recovers at least half of that, its own interval excluding zero; F from the pitch's approach angle beats F from height; the angle effect for hitters with loose timing (contact-depth spread above the median) is at least 1.2 times that for tight timing; whiff rate rises across F quintiles beyond the rates model's prediction. Falsifier: F's coefficient at or below zero, or its gain's interval including zero. Decision rule: if the flexible gain and F's share both hold, the model is frozen (commit and parameters) and scored once on the untouched set (August to September 2026); if it holds there, it enters the matchup engine and the simulator as a per-pitcher, per-hitter whiff adjustment, replayed like any other change. Results (run 37877565820; trained on 309,477 swings of 2025, scored on 225,466 of 2026 through July; whiff rate 23.0%; league contact-depth spread 9.6 in; attack angle steepens 5.5 degrees per foot lower): log loss per swing G0 0.43062, G1 0.42307, G2 0.42204. The hitter's rates add 7.5 nats per 1,000 swings [6.8, 8.2]; **his swing geometry adds 1.04 more [0.72, 1.35]** in the flexible model. The one-parameter mechanism adds 0.11 [0.06, 0.17] (coefficient +0.074 per inch, the falsifier not triggered) but only 11% of the flexible gain, and the competitor built on height does as well (0.14 [0.07, 0.21], 13%); F on top of the flexible geometry -0.33. The mechanism's signature holds: the angle effect is 1.8 times larger for loose-timing hitters (0.88 against 0.49 per unit of sine), and the top F quintiles miss more than the rates model expects (24.1% against 23.0%, 36.4% against 34.8%); on the swing's own attack angle (a mechanism check, partly reaction) the coefficient is 0.36 per inch. Predictions held for the geometry gain, the timing-spread contrast and the quintiles; failed for F's share (11% against at least half) and for approach angle beating height. **Not frozen for the untouched set under the rule.** Reading: how a hitter swings (his usual attack angle, how consistent his timing is) carries real information about his misses beyond his whiff rate, about 1 nat per 1,000 swings; the swing-plane formula with the pitch's approach angle explains only a tenth of it, and approach angle does no better than height. Next (CONTACT-02): which part of the geometry carries it (timing spread, attack angle, their interplay with location and pitch type), with the hitter's own angle variability, tilt and attack direction added. | geometry +1.04 nats/1000 swings; formula 11% of it |
| MATCHUP-03 | MATCHUP-01 for misses. It fits the Decision Horizon's two clocks (the swing committed about 260 ms out, the bat steered until about 110 ms).
