| COMMAND-02 | Command is the scatter of a pitch around where it was aimed, and the aim is not in public data. VALUE-08F's 32 runs assume every pitcher scatters 0.6 ft per axis. Public command measures mix where a pitcher aims with how well he hits it: xCTRL (arXiv 2508.19184) fits a mixture to a pitcher's locations and averages each pitch's distance to the mixture's centers; the Kirby Index measures release-angle spread. New here: when a pitcher throws the same pitch type twice in a row he sometimes aims at the same spot again, and then the change in location between the two pitches is execution only (variance 2 sigma^2 per axis; his lasting miss direction cancels). Over all consecutive same-type pairs in a plate appearance (feed, 2023 to July 2026; pitchouts, intentional balls and bunts out), the location change is a mixture of that narrow same-intent part and a broad changed-intent part. EM per pitcher, season and pitch group (at least 80 pairs) gives sigma per axis. It is an upper bound: re-aiming slightly differently counts as scatter. Checks: (a) the narrow part's posterior share after a two-strike foul (count unchanged, the same aim most likely) against after a foul with fewer strikes (both first pitches fouled, so the same selection); (b) reliability between game halves and between seasons; (c) a pitcher index, each group's sigma over that group's league median weighted by pairs. Prediction tests: (d) next season's walks and hit batters per plate appearance, fitted on 2023 to 2024 and scored on 2024 to 2025 and 2025 to 2026, beyond walk rate (shrunk), zone rate and chase rate; the index is compared with the raw location spread, an xCTRL-style mixture distance and the spread of 3-0 and 3-1 fastballs, and tested beyond the raw spread; (e) within a season, one half's index against the other half's walks, fitted on 2023-2024 and scored on 2025-2026; (f) next season's run value per plate appearance, reported only. Per-pitcher scatter exported for the scouting view (development seasons only). Checked on synthetic seasons first (pitchers with known sigma, intent spots, a repeat-aim chance): recovery correlation 0.55 to 0.59 per cell, with the median estimate 0.94 to 0.97 of the true sigma; reliability between halves 0.35 to 0.41 with the true sigma fixed. No false walk gain in a world where scatter does nothing (+0.0004 [-0.0001, +0.0009]). Foul-matched same-intent check: -0.0003 [-0.0026, +0.0021] with nothing planted and +0.015 [0.013, 0.018] with a higher repeat chance after two-strike fouls. An unmatched first version showed +0.008 with nothing planted; it was fixed before registering | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: (1) the median four-seam sigma is between 0.4 and 0.8 ft per axis, and breaking balls scatter more than four-seamers; (2) check (a) is positive with the interval excluding zero; (3) the index repeats between seasons (correlation at least 0.3); (4) in test (d) the index's squared-error reduction is positive with the interval excluding zero, both beyond walk, zone and chase rates and beyond the raw location spread. Decision rule: if (1) to (3) hold, the repeat scatter is the program's command measure. The scouting view then prices each pitcher's edge at his own scatter (VALUE-15, registered next), and the stated 32 runs carries the measured typical scatter. If (1) fails, the 32 runs are re-priced at the measured scatter by a registered run. If (4) also holds, the index is a candidate pitcher feature for the player model's walk part (a refit, then the replay gates). If (2) fails, the narrow part is not shown to be repeated aims, and the measure is reported as a location measure only Results (run 37910193814; 2,619,246 development pitches, 699,297 consecutive same-type pairs, 27% of pitches in a pair against 49% in the synthetic seasons; 2,640 pitcher-season-group cells): median sigma per axis 0.593 ft for four-seamers (quartiles 0.475 to 0.671), sinkers 0.582, cutters 0.589, sliders 0.568, curveballs 0.589, changeups and splitters 0.563; the narrow part's share is small (median 0.08 to 0.13, against 0.2 to 0.5 planted in the synthetic seasons). **(2) failed: after a two-strike foul the same-intent posterior is +0.0008 [-0.0015, +0.0031] against after earlier fouls. (3) failed: the index correlates 0.08 between game halves and 0.11 between seasons. (4) failed: +0.0004 [-0.042, +0.040].** (1) held for four-seamers only in number (breaking balls do not scatter more), and with (2) and (3) failing the 0.59 is not an execution scatter. Registered comparisons: the raw location spread per pitch type and side (each group's spread over its league median) adds to next-season walks beyond walk, zone and chase rates, **+0.111 [0.037, 0.186] (x1e-4 squared error; test R squared 0.304 to 0.323, 541 pitcher-seasons), coefficient +0.044 walk rate per unit of the index**, repeats 0.69 between seasons, and keeps its gain on the common set (+0.113 [0.037, 0.190]); the xCTRL-style mixture distance adds +0.015 [0.005, 0.025] (0.64 between seasons); the 3-0 and 3-1 fastball spread +0.007 [-0.032, +0.051] (0.24 between seasons). Next-season run value: nothing for either (secondary). **Reading: consecutive same-type pitches rarely repeat an aim closely enough to separate execution from intent, so the repeated-aim identification of command is ruled out with public locations; the 0.6 ft in VALUE-08F stays an assumption.** What does carry forward information on walks is the plain spread of a pitcher's locations within each pitch type (the release-spread idea behind the Kirby Index, not validated publicly before), beyond his walk, zone and chase rates and ahead of the published mixture measure. It is a candidate for the player model's walk part only through a new registration (a refit and the replay gates). | failed: repeated aims do not identify command; location spread predicts walks (+0.11) |
| WARMUP-01 | Whose decline is the times-through-the-order penalty? FATIGUE-01 ruled out lost velocity and EXPOSURE-01 familiarity with a pitcher's pitch types; the closest published analysis (Brill, Deshpande and Wyner, arXiv 2210.06724) finds a continuous decline through a start and says it cannot tell batter learning from fatigue. Three explanations are tangled in a starter's times through: the hitter warming up to live pitching from anyone (general exposure), the hitter learning this pitcher (pair familiarity), and the pitcher tiring (load). Relievers separate them: in a reliever's first meeting with a hitter there is no familiarity and little load, while the hitter may have batted three times; relievers who enter early (short starts, openers, long relief) meet hitters who have batted little. Substitutes come in late and cold. Plate appearances 2023 to July 2026 (feed; outs, runners and score from the plate-appearance history). Talent enters as batter-season and pitcher-season effects estimated from the player's other games (leave-game-out), because earlier rates absorb each player's usual contexts (relievers mostly face hitters who have batted) and effects from the same game carry that game's errors. (S1) Joint run-value model: steps for the hitter's earlier plate appearances in the game (k = 1, 2, 3, 4 or more), his earlier meetings with this pitcher (F = 1, 2 or more), the pitcher's pitches thrown per 100, a substitute's first plate appearance and the hitter's pitches seen beyond 3.9 per earlier plate appearance, with platoon, home, outs, runners and score; game-bootstrap intervals; with inning and in linear form as secondary readings. (S2) Relievers' first meetings only, steps and linear. (S4) Out-of-sample log loss, fitted on 2023-2024 and scored on 2025 and 2026 through July (each and pooled) with earlier rates as talent, as the simulator has it: the decomposition added to the simulator's structure (starter times through by batters faced, reliever, inning). Checked on synthetic seasons first, with early hooks and openers. Planted exposure steps of 0.015, 0.030, 0.045 and 0.045 runs were recovered as 0.007, 0.030, 0.043 and 0.043; familiarity steps of 0.011 and 0.022 as 0.016 and 0.024; the substitute's cold start (-0.037) as -0.040; load (0.011 per 100 pitches) as 0.007. With only familiarity and load planted, the exposure steps were 0.001 to 0.003. In three null worlds, the exposure steps stayed within plus or minus 0.007; one of twelve intervals excluded zero, and the same deviation appears with the true talents, so it belongs to that draw. The S2 slopes were -0.0026, -0.0009 and +0.0003; the substitute term +0.008, -0.002 and +0.014; S4 gains at or below zero. Without early relief, exposure and familiarity were not separable, so the receipt reports how many relief meetings come early in the hitter's game. Dropped before registering: earlier rates as talent (they misattributed the effects) and a substitutes-batting-twice contrast (a second plate appearance happens when the first went well) | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: (1) general exposure: in S2 the linear slope per earlier plate appearance is positive with the interval excluding zero, and in S1 the k = 3 step is positive with the interval excluding zero; (2) a substitute's first plate appearance is worse than his season level says (negative, interval excluding zero); (3) general exposure carries at least half of a starter's rise from the first time through to the third (S1's k = 2 step at least half of the simulator-structure model's third-time term); (4) S4's pooled gain is positive with the interval excluding zero. Decision rule: if (1) and (4) hold, the simulator's times-through structure gets a hitter-exposure layer (steps by the hitter's earlier plate appearances applied against every pitcher, with familiarity and load as measured, and the cold substitute if (2) holds), built and replayed with paired streams under the usual gates; if (1) holds without (4), it is a strategy finding only (a fresh reliever does not reset hitters who have warmed up, so the case for pulling a starter before the third time through is smaller by the exposure share) and the simulator stays as it is; if (1) fails, the decline belongs to the pitcher (familiarity or load), as the simulator assumes Results (run 37917413831; 671,176 plate appearances, relievers 42%; relief meetings by the hitter's earlier plate appearances 2,158, 8,831, 70,539, 121,597 and 38,732 for k = 0 to 4 or more): the simulator-structure model gives the familiar penalty, starters' second time through +0.0082 runs per plate appearance [0.0046, 0.0116] and third +0.0114 [0.0062, 0.0147]. **S1: the pitcher's pitches thrown +0.026 runs per plate appearance per 100 pitches [0.015, 0.038]; earlier meetings with this pitcher F1 -0.0001 [-0.006, +0.007], F2+ +0.0025 [-0.007, +0.011]; the hitter's earlier plate appearances k1 +0.0003, k2 -0.0051 [-0.0090, -0.0014], k3 -0.0095 [-0.0135, -0.0057], k4+ -0.0092 [-0.0141, -0.0042]**; a substitute's first plate appearance -0.0143 [-0.0197, -0.0076]; extra pitches seen nothing. With inning the hitter steps are more negative (k4+ -0.037) and load +0.015 [0.001, 0.021]; linear, -0.0032 per earlier plate appearance [-0.0040, -0.0022]. **S2 (relievers' first meetings): -0.0030 per earlier plate appearance [-0.0050, -0.0013]**; steps +0.010, +0.001, -0.001, -0.003 (all spanning zero); substitute -0.017 [-0.025, -0.010] (linear). **S4: the decomposition adds +0.22 nats per 1,000 plate appearances [0.08, 0.37] (2025), +0.44 [0.28, 0.60] (2026 through July), pooled +0.31 [0.21, 0.42]; relievers' plate appearances +0.47 and +0.79.** **(1) failed (hitters do not warm up: they do slightly worse the more they have batted, against relievers too); (2) held; (3) failed; (4) held.** Under the rule the decline belongs to the pitcher: it follows his pitches thrown, and familiarity with him adds nothing measurable (its interval still reaches the size of the whole penalty, so it is not excluded). Reading: hitters do not get better at hitting as a game goes on, and they do not get better against a pitcher by having seen him; the times-through-the-order penalty tracks how many pitches the pitcher has thrown, as a continuous decline (agreeing with Brill and co-authors, now with the batter-learning explanation tested and rejected). A fresh reliever therefore does reset it, and a starter's third time through is costly because of his pitch count, not the order. A substitute's first plate appearance is 0.014 runs worse than his level, about the cold-bat penalty. S4 says the simulator's structure (times through by batters faced, inning) misses part of this, mostly on relievers; a pitch-count and cold-substitute layer is a separate registration (LOAD-01) with its own replay test. | (1) failed: no hitter warm-up; the penalty follows pitches thrown (+0.026 per 100); cold substitute -0.014; S4 +0.31 |
| LOAD-01 | Decision on WARMUP-01's S4 gain (a pitch-count and exposure layer for the simulator), with a size check before building it, as FATIGUE-01-LAYER did. The simulator already times a starter's decline by batters faced, and starters differ in pitches per batter by about 3.6 to 4.2, so over 25 batters the pitch count that WARMUP-01 says drives the decline differs from the batters-faced clock by about 15 pitches, about 0.004 runs a plate appearance over his last eight batters: about 0.03 runs a game. The hitter steps are a level for late plate appearances that the simulator's inning terms and CTX-02's late-inning offsets already calibrate on average (RUNS-03 found no late-inning excess), and they barely differ between games. Cold substitutes do not exist in the simulator (each lineup slot bats all game). A game-to-game lever of about 0.03 runs is worth a win-Brier change near 0.00001, a thirtieth of the replay test's interval | **Deferred (no build): below the replay test's floor.** WARMUP-01's measurements stand as strategy findings (pull decisions should key on the pitcher's pitch count, not on how many times the order has turned over; a hitter is not more dangerous for having seen a starter; a fresh reliever does reset the decline). If the replay floor drops (a PA-level gate for small layers, or a package of small layers tested together), this is a candidate | deferred: below the replay floor |
| SWINGMAP-01 | Do hitters chase where their bat goes? A hitter's own chase spots at the decision moment (his swing map minus the same-side mean map of the other hitters; MATCHUP-01's representation, VALUE-08's own part) are a lasting trait (MAPS-01) that pitchers do not use (EXPLOIT-01, EXPLOIT-03), but nothing yet says why a hitter chases where he does. Physical reasoning: a bat path that rises steeply through contact matches pitches descending steeply (low pitches), and a pull-side path meets inside pitches out front, so a hitter may be most willing to swing where his swing works. Per hitter-season (2025 and 2026 through July): map features read for a first-pitch four-seamer, in points of swing chance: high minus low (mean own part above the zone, 3.75 and 4.0 ft, minus below, 1.0 and 1.25 ft, across the plate), away minus inside (just off the plate at mid heights), and the overall outside level; maps fitted per season with shrinkage 10 and at least 500 pitches, the league model on all development data. Bat measures from Savant bat tracking, each net of the pitch it met: on competitive swings (bat speed at least 50 mph), a season's regression of the measure on the pitch's height and side (with squares), contact depth, speed, two strikes and pitch group; the hitter's mean residual (at least 150 swings) is his intrinsic attack angle, swing path tilt, pull-side attack direction, contact depth, bat speed and swing length. Attack direction's sign by batter side is set by the data (agreement with where his balls in play go). Correlations by season with intervals over hitters, by batter side for the headline pair, the same for each hitter's change from 2025 to 2026, and how each feature repeats between the seasons. Checked on synthetic seasons first: with the attack angle tied to a lower swing zone, attack angle against high minus low -0.94 and -0.95, and the change against the change -0.61 [-0.69, -0.51]; pull direction against away minus inside -0.87, the orientation recovered (raw +0.33 right, -0.32 left). | CHANGE-01 | Does a hitter's timing carry across a pitching change? TIMING-01 to 03F: hitters do not fully re-time between one pitcher's pitches (23% of a flight-time difference is not re-timed) and the last pitch's speed moves the next contact. If the calibration also carries across a change, a reliever who throws harder than the pitcher he replaces meets late hitters, one who throws softer meets early hitters, for his first batters, fading as they watch him. No public measurement of this was found (closest: Carleton on changing speeds within one pitcher's sequence; Marchi on velocity by situation). Speed gap = the reliever's usual fastball speed (four-seam and sinker on his earlier days of the season, at least 30) minus the mean of the last 10 fastballs (at least 5) of the pitcher he replaced. Plate appearances in relievers' first meetings with each hitter, feed 2023 to July 2026 (development; the untouched months are dropped in code). (R1) run value per plate appearance on the gap for his first three batters, batters 4 to 6 and later ones, with leave-game-out batter-season and pitcher-season effects (WARMUP-01's design: the reliever's own level is absorbed, so the gap acts through who pitched before him), platoon, home, outs, runners, score, inning, his batters faced and pitches thrown, the hitter's earlier plate appearances and substitutes; strikeouts and walks the same way; the previous pitcher's usual speed instead of his last fastballs; the absolute gap. (R3) whiff per swing on the reliever's fastballs in those plate appearances (first two pitches and later), his own speed against his usual, zone and count held. (R4) out-of-sample log loss of the gap terms on a plate-appearance model with both players' earlier rates (fit 2023-2024, scored 2025 and 2026 through July). Checked on synthetic seasons first (relievers faster than starters, faster relievers better, which the pitcher effects must absorb): planted -0.010 runs per mph for the first three batters recovered as -0.0100 [-0.0109, -0.0092] and -0.0110, batters 4 to 6 +0.0003; planted whiffs +0.010 per mph recovered as +0.0099 and +0.0101; with nothing planted (three seeds) the first-three coefficient was +0.0009 [+0.0001, +0.0020], -0.0001 and -0.0001, whiffs -0.0007 to +0.0011, the absolute gap -0.0008 to -0.0014 (and +0.0018 where a signed effect was planted, from the run scale's curvature), batters 7 and later +0.0013 to +0.0018 (who stays in long is selected by how he does). Two designs failed the null check and were dropped: the gap to the pitcher who follows (-0.0017 with nothing planted) and a within-entry gap from each hitter's own last fastballs (+0.0014 to +0.0037) | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: (1) R1's gap for the first three batters is negative (a reliever faster than the pitcher he replaces allows fewer runs), -0.0010 runs per plate appearance per mph or stronger, interval excluding zero; (2) it fades: batters 4 to 6 at most half the first three's size; (3) R3's whiff per swing on his fastballs rises with the gap for the first three batters, interval excluding zero. Decision rule: if (1) and (3) hold, timing carried across a pitching change is a development result, frozen and scored once on the untouched months (CHANGE-01F); if it holds there, a reliever's first batters get a speed-gap term in the simulator after a size check (LOAD-01's way), and the scouting view states what a speed change between pitchers is worth. If (1) fails, hitters re-time fully between pitchers (warm-up pitches and the first looks are enough) and the carryover stays inside one pitcher's sequence. Results (run 37931386910; 190,393 first meetings in 41,819 reliever entries with a gap, 119,894 among the first three batters; gap SD 3.6 mph, middle half -1.9 to +2.6, a quarter of entries 4 mph or more either way; the gap is unrelated to inning and score, correlation -0.03 and -0.01): **run value per mph for the first three batters -0.00004 [-0.00077, +0.00065]**; each of the first three -0.0003, -0.0000, +0.0002; batters 4 to 6 +0.0007 [-0.0004, +0.0016]; with the previous pitcher's usual speed +0.0002 [-0.0003, +0.0006]; absolute gap +0.0006 [-0.0007, +0.0018]; strikeouts -0.00006 [-0.0007, +0.0006]; walks +0.0008 [+0.0002, +0.0011] (a faster reliever walks a little more, the wrong direction for timing and the same for batters 4 to 6, so not the mechanism). **Whiff per swing on his fastballs: first two pitches -0.0002 [-0.0010, +0.0008], later pitches -0.0008 [-0.0013, +0.0000]**, while his own pitch speed against his usual moves whiffs +0.0189 per mph [0.0176, 0.0205] (the check that the pitch-level design sees speed when it matters). Out of sample the gap terms add +0.30 [0.10, 0.50] (2025) and +0.25 [-0.02, +0.54] nats per 1,000 plate appearances to a model of earlier rates, which is the reliever's own speed (the gap correlates 0.69 with it) carrying talent beyond his rates, not timing; the production model already has pitcher velocity. **(1) and (3) failed.** Reading: hitters re-time fully between pitchers. Warm-up pitches watched from the dugout and the on-deck circle and the first look are enough; a 5 mph speed change at a pitching change is worth at most about 0.004 runs per plate appearance to the first three batters either way, and no layer or scouting advice follows. The timing carryover found in TIMING-03F stays inside one pitcher's sequence. | failed: -0.00004 [-0.0008, +0.0006] runs/PA/mph; whiffs -0.0002 |
| STANCE-01 | Is a hitter's sideways chase pattern where he stands? ZONEMAP-01: part of the vertical own part is the hitter's own strike zone (r 0.35), and maps on each batter's zone were credited (MATCHUP-08F). The sideways analog: if hitters judge a pitch against their body rather than the plate, a hitter standing farther off the plate has his swing region shifted toward himself in plate coordinates, so he chases away less and inside more (the body-frame reading); a hitter who stands off the plate and dives in would show the opposite. Savant's batting-stance measures (distance off the plate from its inside edge to the center of mass, depth from the front of the plate) are public only as a leaderboard; here each hitter-season's position is recovered from the intercept fields on every bat-tracked swing (ball minus batter at contact, sideways and toward the pitcher) and the ball's fitted flight: the position that makes the ball's sideways position at (batter depth + intercept depth) minus the sideways intercept agree across his swings (a grid over depth, identified by how the flight's sideways slope varies across his pitches), so it is his position at contact, after the stride. The sideways sign for each batter side is set by the data. SWINGMAP-01's design otherwise (maps per season with shrinkage 10, at least 500 pitches; first-pitch four-seamer reads; away minus inside, outside level and high minus low in points of swing chance; Savant 2025 and 2026 through July, at least 150 swings), correlations with bootstrap intervals by season and within hitter from 2025 to 2026. Checked on synthetic seasons first (sideways intercept mirrored for left-handed hitters, which the code found): distance off the plate recovered with split-half reliability 0.997 and the right median (26.3 inches against 26.4 planted), depth more loosely (split-half 0.39 to 0.50); with the body-frame effect planted, distance against away minus inside -0.91 in both seasons and its change against the change -0.71; with nothing planted -0.08 [-0.19, +0.04] and -0.11 [-0.22, +0.00], change -0.07 [-0.18, +0.04] (the same hitters in both seasons, so one chance draw), depth against away minus inside +0.10 and +0.18 [0.07, 0.30] (chance among about twenty pairs; depth is exploratory) | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: (1) distance off the plate against away minus inside is negative, r at or below -0.2 with the interval excluding zero, in both seasons; (2) within hitter, the change in distance from 2025 to 2026 against the change in away minus inside is negative with the interval excluding zero; (3) exploratory: depth against the outside level is negative (deeper hitters see more of the flight before the decision). Decision rule: if (1) holds with r at or below -0.5, the sideways own part is substantially where the hitter stands: MATCHUP-09 (the league model and maps with each pitch's sideways position measured from the batter, the edge re-priced) is registered next and the scouting view says so; between -0.2 and -0.5, the same registration with the view noting it as partial; if r is +0.2 or more with the interval excluding zero in both seasons, stance explains part of it in the diving direction (registered the same way, read as compensation, not perception); otherwise hitters' sideways chase spots are not where they stand and the plate is the frame. Results (run 37932692432; 318,247 and 216,480 bat-tracked swings; 417 and 370 hitter-seasons with maps): the data set the sideways sign (right-handed hitters' center of mass at -3.05 ft from the plate's center, left-handed at +2.98 with the intercept mirrored for them, as in the synthetic check); **distance off the plate at contact: median 28.0 inches (2025) and 27.9 (2026), middle half 26.3 to 29.3, split-half reliability 0.994, season to season 0.89**; depth at contact median 28 to 29 inches behind the front of the plate but loosely identified (split-half 0.59, season to season 0.60) and at the grid's deep edge for a quarter of hitter-seasons (23% and 26%, excluded; the grid stopped 35 inches back), leaving 354 and 310 hitters. **(1) distance off the plate against away minus inside -0.266 [-0.364, -0.160] (2025) and -0.304 [-0.407, -0.185] (2026), slope -0.75 and -0.77 points of swing chance per inch; right-handed hitters -0.31 and -0.39, left-handed -0.23 and -0.23. (2) Within hitter, the change in distance against the change in away minus inside -0.311 [-0.446, -0.186] (297 hitters).** (3) depth against the outside level -0.06 and -0.05, spanning zero. Unregistered: distance against high minus low +0.20 [0.08, 0.31] and +0.10 [-0.02, +0.22]. **(1) held in the partial band and (2) held; (3) not supported.** Reading: hitters judge a pitch's side against their own body, not the plate. A hitter who stands an inch farther off the plate chases about 0.75 points less away relative to inside, and hitters who moved off the plate between seasons shifted their chase pattern inside by about the same rate, so a stance change announces a chase-pattern change before the swing data piles up. Where he stands accounts for about a tenth of the spread in the sideways own part (r squared 0.07 to 0.09; the pattern repeats 0.72 between seasons). Under the rule MATCHUP-09 (the league model and maps with each pitch's sideways position measured from the batter, the edge re-priced) is registered next, and the scouting view notes it as partial. | held (partial): r -0.27 and -0.30; within hitter -0.31 |
| WHERE-01 | Where does the closing line still beat our own model? Alec's goal is to beat every model that exists, and the closing line is the strongest public one. Production replays with the PROD-03 settings (v2-prod3-1000-2025 and -2026, 1,000 worlds per game), our model taught on the other season's closing lines (taught-v5's two inputs, simulator and team-model log-odds), closing lines (2025 SportsBookReview through August 16, 2026 ESPN), 4,140 games. Per game, Brier of ours minus the market's, by month, the starters' earlier batters faced, favorite size, the size and direction of the disagreement; and a logistic of results on both log-odds (whose information the results follow), date-bootstrap intervals (scratch harness/where_gap.py) | Diagnostic on development seasons (the settings were chosen on them). **Brier pooled: market 0.24289, ours 0.24245, the simulator alone 0.24256; 2025 0.24235, 0.24146, 0.24230; 2026 0.24325, 0.24313, 0.24274.** Ours minus market pooled -0.00044 [-0.00165, +0.00072]. Results logistic on both, pooled: market 0.38 [-0.03, 0.75], ours 0.65 [0.24, 1.09] (2025 0.33 and 0.66, 2026 0.46 and 0.60). No segment where the market's edge gathers: every segment's interval spans zero and nothing runs monotonically with the starters' data, the month, the favorite size or the disagreement. Headline weights market/ours on the same games: 60/40 0.241464 (2025) and 0.242936 (2026), 50/50 0.241345 and 0.242912, 40/60 0.241271 and 0.242910. **Reading: with the production simulator our own number is level with the closing line on these seasons, and when both are combined the results lean on ours.** Part of that is in-sample (these seasons tuned the settings), so the headline stays at 60/40 (moving toward ours gains 0.00002 to 0.00019 on the seasons that chose the settings) and the forward record decides (FWD-02). | diagnostic: ours level with the close; no segment to fix |
| FWD-02 | Our own number against the market, forward in time: every game from October 9, 2026 (the rest of the 2026 postseason, then 2027), each scored with the version in force at its first pitch: Brier of our model (taught, no market input), of the market's last captured pregame line (the several-books average when at least three books are read) and of the headline; the logistic of results on our and the market's log-odds; paired by date. Power: one season of about 2,430 games has a standard error near 0.0006 on the Brier difference, so a season can only show a lead of about 0.0012; the postseason alone is a record, not a test | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before any of its games.** Predictions: over 2027, ours minus the market at or below zero (point estimate) and the headline below the market; by the end of 2027 the results logistic gives ours a positive weight with the interval excluding zero. Decision rule: if ours is below the market over the full 2027 season, the headline's weights move toward ours by a registered refit; if ours is worse by more than 0.0006, the settings chosen on 2026 are re-examined for in-sample tuning. | registered |
| MATCHUP-09 | STANCE-01's rule: the sideways coordinate partly measured from the batter. The credited representation (MATCHUP-01F, with batter zones for the league model, MATCHUP-08F) reads where the pitch appears headed against the plate; STANCE-01 found hitters judge its side partly against their body (a hitter an inch farther off the plate chases about 0.75 points less away relative to inside). Each pitch's sideways position is moved by w times the hitter's distance off the plate beyond the average hitter's (positions from the 2025 intercept fields, STANCE-01's estimator with the depth grid widened to 53 inches back; hitters without a position keep the plate frame), so a farther hitter sees every pitch farther away. w is chosen among 0.25, 0.5 and 1 on the validation half by the league model's log loss. MATCHUP-01's development design otherwise (league model on location, controls and pitcher propensity at the decision moment; hitter maps with the chosen shrinkage; log loss on the test season's decisions, game-clustered intervals), in two tests: trained on 2023-2024 and scored on 2025, and trained on 2023-2025 and scored on 2026 through July. Checked on synthetic seasons first (positions and intercepts as in STANCE-01's world): with swings following the body-mixed frame at w 0.5, validation chose 0.5 and the league model gained +2.22 nats per 1,000 decisions [1.94, 2.48], hitter maps +0.52 [0.37, 0.67]; with swings following the plate, the chosen weight lost -0.58 [-0.73, -0.43] for the league model and hitter maps were unchanged (-0.00) | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the runs.** Predictions: (1) the chosen body-mixed frame beats the plate frame for the league model, interval excluding zero, in both tests; (2) with hitter maps the gain is smaller than the league model's (the maps already carry each hitter's usual position). Decision rule: if (1) holds in both tests, MATCHUP-09F (the untouched months, frozen code, one scoring) is registered, and if credited the league model takes the body-mixed sideways coordinate for hitters with a position; if hitter maps also gain with the interval excluding zero in both tests, the maps follow after the same frozen scoring and the edge is re-priced (VALUE-17); if (1) fails, the plate frame stays in the model and STANCE-01 stands as a description. Results (runs 37934640198 and 37934729243; 463 hitters with a 2025 position, 84% of decisions; the widened grid left 0.2% of fits at its edge and raised depth's split-half reliability to 0.69, distance off the plate 0.992): the plate frame had the best validation log loss in both tests (the smallest body weight, 0.25, was chosen among the body weights); **league model, body-mixed over plate: -0.26 [-0.37, -0.14] (2025) and -0.20 [-0.36, -0.06] (2026 through July) at weight 0.25, -2.3 and -2.0 at 0.5, -10.8 and -9.4 at 1; with hitter maps +0.07 [+0.01, +0.14] and -0.01 [-0.09, +0.05]**; hitter maps over the league +11.7 and +12.5; pair chase slopes 0.97 and 1.12. **(1) failed in both tests.** Reading: where a hitter stands goes with his sideways chase pattern (STANCE-01), but it is not a shift of the whole swing surface: moving every pitch's sideways position by his distance makes the league model worse even at a quarter weight, and his own map already carries the pattern. The plate frame stays; STANCE-01 stands as a description and as a scouting signal (a stance change announces a chase-pattern change). | failed: plate frame stays (-0.26 and -0.20 for the league model) |
| BENCH-01 | Does the decision-moment model beat the strongest flexible learner given the same inputs? (Alec: the goal is to beat every model that exists.) MATCHUP-01F's +40 nats per 1,000 decisions was measured against the standard hitter heat map in the same logistic framework. A gradient-boosted classifier (scikit-learn histogram boosting, 63 leaves, early stopping, up to 400 rounds, 1.5 million training decisions) gets every measured input of the pitch (release and plate speed, movement, spin, release point and extension, the true crossing), the count, pitch group, hands and both players' earlier swing levels, so it could learn the decision-moment projection itself (a function of those inputs). G0: boosting on those inputs; G1: G0 plus the standard hitter heat map (logistic league and hitter maps on the true crossing, as a log-odds input); ours: MATCHUP-01's logistic league and hitter maps at the decision moment; G2: G1 plus ours as an input. Map inputs are cross-fitted on the training rows (two folds by game) and fitted on all training rows for the test. Log loss on the test season's decisions, game-clustered intervals; two tests: trained on 2023-2024 and scored on 2025, and trained on 2023-2025 and scored on 2026 through July. Checked on synthetic seasons first: with swings read at the decision moment, ours beat G1 by +3.6 [3.1, 4.1] and G2 beat G1 by +3.9 [3.4, 4.4]; with swings read on the true crossing, ours lost to G1 by -36.3 and G2 beat G1 by only +0.19 [0.07, 0.32] (a flexible learner gains a little from any extra map input) | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the runs.** Predictions: (1) ours beats G1 with the interval excluding zero in both tests; (2) G2 beats G1 by at least 0.5 nats per 1,000 decisions with the interval excluding zero in both tests. Decision rule: if (1) and (2) hold, the decision-moment model beating a flexible learner with the same inputs is a development result and BENCH-01F scores it once on the untouched months; if only (2) holds, the representation adds to flexible learners and the best pitch model is boosting with it as an input; if neither, a flexible learner with the same inputs already captures it and the credited gains were against a weaker baseline, which the program page must then say. Results (runs 37935421780 and 37935503301; 704,092 and 480,636 test decisions; boosting used all 400 rounds): log loss G0 0.44197 and 0.44554, G1 0.43349 and 0.43708, G2 0.43061 and 0.43406, ours 0.45722 and 0.46003, the logistic heat map 0.49374 and 0.49769. **Ours against G1 -23.7 [-24.3, -23.1] (2025) and -22.9 [-23.7, -22.3] (2026 through July); G2 over G1 +2.88 [2.58, 3.20] and +3.03 [2.68, 3.39]**; G1 over G0 +8.5 in both; ours over the logistic heat map +36.5 and +37.7 (MATCHUP-01's own comparison, reproduced). **(1) failed, (2) held in both tests.** Reading: most of MATCHUP-01F's +40 was the weakness of the logistic league model. A boosted learner given the pitch's measured inputs learns where the league swings without being told about the decision moment, and beats our logistic model by 23 nats. What the decision-moment representation adds beyond the best flexible learner with the standard heat map is the hitter-specific part read at the decision moment: about +3 nats per 1,000 decisions in both seasons. Under the rule the best swing model is boosting with the heat map and the decision-moment maps as inputs (G2), and the program page and scouting view say what the +40 was measured against. | (2) held: +2.9 and +3.0 beyond boosting with the heat map; (1) failed: the logistic model is 23 nats behind boosting |

| SEQ-02 | Does the timing a hitter carries after a called strike reach the outcome of the next pitch? TIMING-03F (credited on untouched contacts): after a called strike the hitter meets the next pitch as if expecting the other family (contact farther out front the slower the strike was, +0.15 inches per 10 ms); after a miss his swing's timing persists; a foul carries nothing; after a taken ball almost nothing (+0.03). If the carried timing reaches outcomes, repeating the previous family on the pitch after a called strike should draw more misses than changing it, beyond what the pitch itself predicts, and the same contrast after a taken ball should be near zero. SEQ-01 asked a different question (how unexpected the pitch type is given the pitcher's habits) and failed. Design (feed; train 2023-2024, test 2025 and 2026 through July; the untouched months stay out): whiff on a swing with a previous pitch in the plate appearance, a logistic model with the pitch's location on the true crossing, group, speed, two strikes, both players' earlier whiff levels, platoon, the count, the previous pitch's family and outcome kind (ball, called strike, foul, miss), plus the sequence terms: each previous kind times repeat-family and times the speed change; game-bootstrap intervals on the training fit; out-of-sample log loss gain of the sequence terms, and the observed-minus-predicted whiff rate for repeats against changes after each previous kind; launch speed on balls in play the same way; boosting with and without the raw sequence facts as a flexible check. Checked on synthetic plate appearances first: a planted +0.30 log-odds on repeats after called strikes was recovered at +0.29 [0.28, 0.32] (contrast against after-ball +0.29 [0.26, 0.35]; observed minus predicted, repeat minus change, +0.053 after called strikes and +0.007 after balls; out of sample +0.39 nats per 1,000 swings); with nothing planted the contrast was -0.02 [-0.04, +0.02] and every after-kind reading within 0.005 | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: (1) called-strike-times-repeat minus ball-times-repeat is positive with the interval excluding zero; (2) in both test seasons the observed-minus-predicted whiff rate for repeats minus changes after a called strike is at least +0.01 (one miss per 100 swings) and larger than after a taken ball; (3) the sequence terms gain out of sample in both test seasons with the interval excluding zero. Decision rule: if (1) and (2) hold, the sequencing rule (after a called strike, come back with the same family) is a development result, frozen and scored once on the untouched months (SEQ-02F), and if credited it enters the matchup report as a sequencing note with its measured size; if (1) fails, the carried timing does not reach misses at a size the data can see, and the sequence finding stays a contact-depth result. Results (run 37950526747; 279,178 test swings in 2025 and 189,959 in 2026 through July): sequence coefficients (log-odds of a miss, training fit): repeat of the family after a taken ball +0.106 [0.087, 0.128], **after a called strike +0.103 [0.072, 0.129]**, after a foul -0.012 [-0.025, +0.024], after a miss +0.209 [0.157, 0.257]; a faster next pitch (per 10 mph) after a ball -0.148, after a called strike -0.040, after a foul -0.045, after a miss -0.124. **(1) failed: called strike minus ball -0.002 [-0.047, +0.022].** (2) after a called strike the repeat-minus-change observed-minus-predicted whiff rate was +0.012 (2025) and +0.016 (2026), at the +0.01 floor, but not larger than after a ball (+0.021 and +0.017); after a miss +0.030 and +0.035; after a foul -0.001. (3) held: the sequence terms gain +0.45 [0.35, 0.55] and +0.44 [0.33, 0.55] nats per 1,000 swings out of sample (boosting with the raw sequence facts +0.83 and +0.85). Launch speed: a repeat after a ball -0.7 mph, after a called strike -0.7, after a miss -0.5, after a foul -0.1. **Under the rule the registered mechanism (the carried timing after a called strike) is not what reaches misses: the whiff effect is the same after a taken ball, where the contact-depth carryover was near zero.** What the data show instead, unregistered: after any taken pitch, and twice as much after a miss, repeating the previous family draws more misses and softer contact than changing it, beyond the pitch itself, while a foul resets it. That is a different, broader sequencing effect; it is registered as SEQ-03F and scored once on the untouched months before any use. | failed as registered; a broader repeat-after-take effect found (+0.10 log-odds; after a miss +0.21), SEQ-03F registered |
| SEQ-03F | The broader sequencing effect SEQ-02 found, scored once on the untouched months: after a taken pitch (ball or called strike) and after a miss, repeating the previous family draws more misses than changing it, beyond the pitch itself; after a foul nothing carries. SEQ-02's design (seq2_study) with final_eval: every model fitted on regular-season swings before August 1, 2026 (2023 on), scored on the swings from August 1 to September 27, 2026; the frozen commit is the one that adds this row | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run; one scoring.** Predictions on the untouched swings: (1) the observed-minus-predicted whiff rate for repeats minus changes is at least +0.01 after a taken ball, after a called strike and after a miss, and within 0.01 of zero after a foul; (2) the sequence terms gain out of sample with the interval excluding zero. Decision rule: if both hold, the sequencing effect is credited at the training coefficients' size (about +0.10 log-odds of a miss on a repeat after a take, +0.21 after a miss; about 1.5 to 3 points of whiff rate) and enters the matchup report as a sequencing note and the plate-appearance planner (PLAN-01) as a state-dependent miss adjustment; if (1) fails for the takes, the repeat effect stands only after misses (or not at all) as the untouched data say. Results (run 37953062671, frozen 4ec64b68; 89,898 untouched swings with a previous pitch): observed-minus-predicted whiff rate, repeat minus change: **after a called strike +0.025, after a taken ball +0.019, after a miss +0.034, after a foul -0.004**; the sequence terms gain +0.60 [0.42, 0.77] nats per 1,000 swings (boosting with the raw sequence facts +1.00 [0.68, 1.33]). Training coefficients (log-odds of a miss, fitted on 2023 to July 2026): repeat after a ball +0.124 [0.096, 0.138], after a called strike +0.112 [0.090, 0.138], after a foul -0.000, after a miss +0.216 [0.180, 0.235]; a faster next pitch per 10 mph after a ball -0.150, after a called strike -0.040, after a foul -0.057, after a miss -0.119; launch speed on a repeat -0.70 mph after a ball, -0.60 after a called strike, -0.44 after a miss, -0.16 after a foul. **Both predictions held. Credited: after a taken pitch, and more after a miss, coming back with the same family draws more misses and softer contact than changing it; a slower next pitch adds to it; a foul resets it.** It enters the matchup report as a sequencing note and the planner (PLAN-02, to be registered) as the state-dependent miss adjustment at these coefficients. | credited: +0.019 to +0.034 whiff rate on repeats after takes and misses; +0.60 nats |
| PLAN-01 | The plate appearance as a sequential decision problem (Alec's direction: a new framework, October 9). Existing tools, ours included, score pitches one at a time against a fixed map. Here the state is the count and what the hitter just saw (the previous pitch's family and outcome: ball, called strike, foul, miss; 13 states by count), the actions are the pitcher's own pitches to that side (type and spot, a sample of his training pitches per count group), the transition model is the credited pieces (swing at the decision moment with the league's family part and the hitter's family maps, MATCHUP-05F; miss on the true crossing with his whiff map, MATCHUP-03/BENCH-02F; league called-strike and foul models; a linear run-value model for balls in play by location, type, speed, count, platoon and both players' contact levels), and the value is the plate appearance's run value, solved backward from strike three and ball four by value iteration (fouls at two strikes loop). Three values per pair: the pitcher's own mix (his count-group frequencies), the league plan (optimized without the maps; it still knows the hitter's swing, miss and contact levels) evaluated under the hitter's models, and the hitter plan (optimized with the maps). Claims: runs per plate appearance saved by the league plan over the mix and by the hitter plan over the league plan. The test, because a claimed value from the hitter's own maps can be noise-chasing: the natural experiment of VALUE-08. On the test seasons' actual pitches of the most frequent pairs, the hitter-specific advantage of the pitch actually thrown (its Q under the hitter plan minus the mix's average, minus the same under the league plan) against the pitch's realized run value (the next count state's value from the training seasons, or the terminal outcome, minus the current state's), with the league advantage held; pitchers do not aim at the maps (EXPLOIT-01), so the slope measures whether the plan's hitter-specific differences are real, and the validated value is the slope times the claim; placebo with another same-side hitter's maps. Code plan_study (tools/brl_discovery.py); train 2023-2024, test 2025 and 2026 through July; the untouched months stay out. Checked on synthetic plate appearances first (pitch-by-pitch worlds with count progression): with hitter-specific swing shifts and miss holes planted, the hitter-specific slope was +0.40 [0.21, 0.63] and +0.37 [0.17, 0.60] on two test seasons (the claim's noise is why it is below one; the validated value is the slope times the claim) and the placebo -0.11 [-0.25, +0.07]; with no hitter-specific structure the claimed hitter-plan value was still +14.9 runs per team-season (maps fitted to noise) while the slope was +0.07 [-0.57, +0.55], which is the guard this test provides | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: (1) the hitter-specific slope is positive with the interval excluding zero in both test seasons; (2) the placebo slope's interval includes zero and its point estimate is below half of (1)'s; (3) the league plan's advantage slope is positive with the interval excluding zero (the chain's count-level choices are real). Decision rule: if (1) and (2) hold, the planner is a development result and PLAN-01F (frozen, one scoring on the untouched months, the same test) is registered; if credited there, the report shows what to throw next by state for each pair, with the hitter-specific value stated as the untouched slope times the claim; if (1) fails, the hitter-specific plan adds nothing the test can see and only the aim plan (VALUE-08F) stands. The sequencing adjustment (SEQ-03F) is zero in this run and enters a later registration if credited. Results (run 37954881883; 4,834 pairs solved, 98,816 actual pitches of 2025 and 57,855 of 2026 through July in the natural experiment): claimed values per plate appearance, the pitcher's mix -0.206, the league plan -0.225, the hitter plan -0.237 (league plan over the mix 0.019 runs per plate appearance, 115 per team-season; hitter plan over the league plan 0.012, 76 per team-season). **(1) held: the hitter-specific slope +0.40 [0.25, 0.52] (2025) and +0.28 [0.11, 0.45] (2026); the league advantage slope +0.43 and +0.42.** (3) held. **(2) failed: the placebo (another same-side hitter's maps) gave -0.25 [-0.38, -0.14], an interval excluding zero on the wrong side**, which the rule did not anticipate (the synthetic effect world had given -0.11 [-0.25, +0.07]). Reading: the hitter-specific differences the plan makes are real in the sense that pitches the plan favors for this hitter went better than pitches it does not, in both seasons, at 0.3 to 0.4 of the claimed size; but a test whose placebo is reliably negative is not understood, and under the rule the planner is not advanced to the untouched months. The claims (115 and 76 runs) are model potential at face value and are not to be stated anywhere; the league plan's slope of 0.43 says even the count-level choices are worth under half of what the chain claims. PLAN-01b (registered below) runs the placebo in several forms and measures the maps' reliability before anything further. | (1) held: slope +0.40 and +0.28; (2) failed: placebo -0.25; not advanced |
| PLAN-01b | Why is PLAN-01's placebo negative, and how much of the claim is map noise? Same models and pairs as PLAN-01 (the development run), the natural-experiment regression repeated with the hitter-specific advantage recomputed under other map sets on a 150,000-pitch sample: 'shuffle' (another same-side hitter's maps, PLAN-01's placebo), 'mean' (the same-side mean maps: the shared shape only), 'noise' (random maps with each hitter's own-part variance), 'flip' (the own part negated, which should reverse the real slope), 'half_a' and 'half_b' (maps fitted on 2023 alone and on 2024 alone; their correlation across pitches is the reliability that attenuates the slope a noisy claim can reach). Each reports its slope, its interval and its correlation with the real advantage | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: (a) 'noise' within [-0.1, +0.1]; (b) 'flip' negative, near minus the real slope; (c) 'mean' explains the shuffle's sign if its slope is negative (the shared shape under-weighted by the league plan) and the shuffle's correlation with the real advantage is near zero. Decision rule: if (a) holds and the shuffle's sign is explained by 'mean' or by a negative correlation with the real advantage, PLAN-01's slope stands as attenuated evidence (the claim scaled by the slope, which the reliability check bounds) and PLAN-01F is registered with 'noise' as the placebo; if (a) fails (random maps give a slope beyond 0.1 either way), the natural-experiment regression for plans is biased and the planner is set aside until a sequential off-policy evaluation with overlap diagnostics replaces it (the audit's recommended design). Results (run 37957704310; 120,000-pitch samples, 2025 and 2026 development; 4,821 pairs): real slopes 2025 0.385 [0.255, 0.503], 2026 0.262 [0.093, 0.440]; **'noise' (random maps with each hitter's own-part variance) -0.357 [-0.496, -0.223], far outside the registered band of 0.1 either way**; 'shuffle' -0.193 [-0.307, -0.069] (its correlation with the real advantage 0.16); 'mean' +0.521 [0.192, 0.850] (the shared shape alone carries a positive slope, so it does not explain the shuffle's sign); 'flip' -0.818 [-0.933, -0.691] (negative as predicted, twice the real slope's size); 'half_a' and 'half_b' 0.317 each, their advantages correlated 0.677 across pitches (reliability). (a) failed, (b) held in sign, (c) failed. **Under the rule set in advance the natural-experiment regression for plans is biased (maps of pure noise move the slope by a third of a run per run of claimed advantage, in the wrong direction) and the planner is set aside.** No plan value is stated. PLAN-03 is the replacement design when taken up: sequential off-policy evaluation of the planner's pitch distribution against the pitcher's logged choices (the behavior policy's propensities from his own mix by state, importance weights with overlap diagnostics and effective sample sizes, the telescoping pitch value as the reward), checked first in the synthetic worlds of VALUE-17 where a plan's true value can be computed. | failed: noise maps give -0.36; planner set aside |
| TOTALS-03 | The expected total taught by results: the simulator's mean total combined with the market's total line. TOTALS-02 found the market line the better single number; WHERE-01's totals check (PROD-03 replays) found our mean total level with the line in 2026 and behind in 2025, and a results regression of the actual total on both gives the simulator a weight in both seasons (0.42 and 0.22, the line 0.83 and 0.72). Fixed cross-season weights, 0.32 simulator and 0.78 line, with the intercept that centers the pooled seasons (-0.543); scratch harness/totals_taught.py on the v2-prod3-1000 replays, 2,454 games of 2026 and 1,686 of 2025 with a closing total | Squared error of the expected total against the actual total, taught minus line, paired by date: **2026 -0.27 [-0.42, -0.13] (19.43 against 19.73); 2025 -0.12 [-0.36, +0.11] (19.96 against 20.10)**, each season with the intercept from the other. Over chances stay unpredictable: a normal around the taught mean scores 0.2508 and 0.2543 against the market's 0.2498 and 0.2497 and a coin's 0.25, so the page states no over chance of its own. **Adopted as a product call (rule 3): the page's expected total is the taught number from October 10, 2026 when a market total is captured, with the simulator's own mean beside it; one season's interval excludes zero, the other's includes it, both negative.** Development seasons; the forward record scores it from October 10. | product call: -0.27 (2026), -0.12 (2025) squared error against the line |
| AUDIT-01 | An outside research audit of the public repository at commit 9fb395f (October 9, 2026; read-only, no sealed-data rerun), received through Alec. Its findings, accepted: (a) our model's 0.24245 is a market-taught number, not market-free, and 'level with the market' is a development-replay similarity with an interval crossing zero; (b) CLV-03 assumes the starters were known at the open and selects disagreements over 0.1 log-odds, so the forward record must keep actual opening snapshots, the denominator, no-move handling and signed magnitude; (c) August to September 2026 is reused validation, not untouched; (d) the 32 runs is policy potential under assumptions; four code findings in value2_study: later pitches of the plate appearance enter the controls, a scattered pitch crossing the zone edge keeps its aim's coefficient, the aim menus come from the evaluation period, and the interval does not propagate map, ranking, command and policy uncertainty; (e) 260 ms is an effective horizon under the model, not a perceptual fact; (f) the planner should be compared as a ladder (historical mix, greedy one pitch, count only, sequence aware) with an absorbing-state solver and abstention where support is poor; (g) calibration must be conditional (count, hands, family, coverage, era) and dependence handled; (h) totals need a joint-distribution diagnosis (first five innings against full games, team against combined runs, tails, extras, park and weather subsets, PIT and CRPS) and a negative-binomial challenger before features; (i) the ABS claim needs the initial call, the challenge and the final ruling split and the Savant plane change reconciled by source; (j) engineering: a machine-readable registration record per experiment, documentation generated from it, research checks before publication, scientific failure-mode tests with false-positive rates and coverage over many repetitions, a smaller discovery module, a locked environment, Actions pinned by commit, a reproduction package, a license and a citation file; (k) product: two options by count, the intended target with its landing cloud and the dangerous miss, why the pitcher can execute it, an insufficient-evidence state, three grades kept apart, structured coach overrides, a crossover pilot. Closest prior work it names: PitchingBot and Stuff+ (component diagnostics; not every incumbent is hitter-agnostic), Driveline's individualized swing decisions (personal swing-versus-take value predates ours), Sutton-Brown 2025 (the closest apparent-location precedent; full text not obtained, so no first-ever claim), Saijo, Fukuda and Kashino 2025 and Higuchi 2016 (occlusion experiments), Douglas 2021 (optimal pitching as a stochastic game), Prasad (sequence graphs), xCTRL (intent against execution), Powers and Yurko 2025 and Brill, Deshpande and Wyner. Not available from here: logged intent, randomized plans in live batting practice, video, a coach pilot | Done the same day: public wording narrowed on the site (market-taught weights, this game's line never seen, development replays, assumed starters, reused months, policy potential, retrospective reconstructions, three grades), the record names its three streams, METHODS rewritten, README points to BRL, evidence rule 6 added. Registered next, in the audit's order: VALUE-17 (the adversarial stress test of the run-value estimator), TOTALS-04 (the distribution diagnosis), PLAN-02 (the planner ladder with the SEQ-03F adjustment), a registration record format (ENG-01). The occlusion design, intent logging and the coach pilot wait for a team. | accepted; wording fixed; four registrations follow |
| VALUE-17 | Stress test of the run-value estimator and the aiming figure in synthetic worlds with known truth (the audit's priority 2). tools/brl_synth.py generates seasons pitch by pitch (count progression, a league swing model with each hitter's own shifts, levels and miss holes, pitchers with their own spots who may target or pitch away from a hitter's spot, foul, called strike, ball-in-play damage), 2023-2024 as map seasons and 2025 as the test season, 200,000 plate appearances, 240 hitters, 120 pitchers; eight kinds: null (hitters swing alike, so any coefficient is a false positive), level (hitters differ only in how much they swing), effect, small (40% of effect's spreads, nearer the real own-part spread), target (every pitcher aims 30% of pitches at the hitter's true chase spot), opposing (half target it, half pitch away), coincident (the miss hole sits on the chase spot), noisy (three times the hitters on the same pitches). The generator prices the truth: per point of a hitter's own part, 0.01 times (value if swing minus value if take) under its own probabilities and count values; and the true value of the estimator's actual aim choices (the chosen third's hitter-specific expected value against the pool's, the generator's oracle choice beside it). value2_study runs on each world with the audit's four code findings as options, all on: the 'pitch' estimand (each pitch's realized telescoping value, only earlier pitches of the plate appearance as controls) beside the 'pa' estimand; aim menus from the pitcher's training-season spots; a scattered pitch priced by the side of the zone it crosses ('side') and by the distance band it crosses in ('bands', six bands, the regression split by band). Smoke results before registration (one 60,000-plate-appearance effect world, local, disclosed): the own-part coefficient came out -0.00112 (pitch estimand; -0.00143 pa) against a truth of -0.00084 per true point, and 1.25 true points per fitted point (shrinkage), so the fitted part's one-standard-deviation value was 82% of the truth's; **the aiming figure (coefficient times the fitted gain) read -63.5 runs per 6,200 at zero scatter and -41.2 at 0.6 ft against true values of the chosen spots of -42.2 and -21.9 (ratios 1.5 and 1.9); priced by side -31.4 against -24.4 (1.3), by band -33.4 against -24.4 (1.4); the oracle -85.3 and -54.1.** The cause, measured: the run value per point differs across pitches, and the chosen spots (where a hitter's own part is largest, near his region's edge) carry a smaller value per point than the regression's average (-0.00069 against -0.00095 per fitted point at the test pitches), with the scatter adding sign flips the side pricing only partly catches | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the lane runs (ten worlds per kind, seeds 100-109).** Predictions: (1) null: the own, within-hitter, neighbor-held and placebo intervals exclude zero in at most 10% of worlds for each estimand (false-positive rate at the nominal 5%, allowing for ten reps); (2) effect and small: the pitch estimand's one-standard-deviation value is within 25% of the truth's in both kinds, and the pa estimand's own coefficient is larger in magnitude than the pitch estimand's in at least eight of ten worlds (the plate appearance's other pitches inflate it); (3) effect: the points figure at 0.6 ft overstates the true value of the chosen spots by a ratio between 1.3 and 2.0 on average, and neither the side nor the band pricing brings the ratio under 1.2; (4) target, opposing, coincident: the pitch estimand's one-standard-deviation value within 25% of effect's (the design's controls carry pitcher targeting), placebos nil (at most 10% excluding zero); (5) noisy: a smaller one-standard-deviation value than effect's with intervals still excluding zero in at least eight of ten worlds. Decision rule: if (1) fails, every ledger row that rests on the coefficient's interval (VALUE-08F, VALUE-11, VALUE-15, VALUE-16) carries a false-positive note and the stated interval is widened by the null's observed spread; if (3) holds, the stated 32 runs is relabeled as the regression's figure with the synthetic overstatement factor beside it until VALUE-18 (structural pricing: the per-point value computed at each aim from the engine's whiff, called-strike, foul and contact components and the count values, not one regression coefficient) is registered and tested in these same worlds; if (2) fails for the pitch estimand, the estimand itself is at fault and the structural pricing becomes the only stated figure. Receipts: research/discovery-value_synth-<run>.json, one per kind; no sealed data is read. Results (runs 37962478538 null, 37962521079 level, 37962566027 effect, 37962608258 small, 37962651070 target, 37962694526 opposing, 37962740015 coincident, 37962784613 noisy; ten worlds each, both estimands): **(1) failed for the plate-appearance estimand's own-map coefficient, which excluded zero in 3 of 10 null worlds (mean +0.00025, spread 0.00055 per point across worlds); held for everything else: the plate-appearance estimand's within-hitter neighbor-held coefficient 1 of 10 and placebo 0 of 10, the pitch estimand's own 1 of 10, neighbor-held 1 of 10, placebo 0 of 10.** (2) held on size, failed on the ordering: the pitch estimand's one-standard-deviation value was 85% of the truth's in effect (-0.0088 against -0.01035) and 85% in small (-0.00373 against -0.0044); the plate-appearance own coefficient exceeded the pitch estimand's in 7 of 10 worlds in both kinds (8 to 10 of 10 in the other kinds). **(3) held on the points figure: in effect worlds the regression figure at 0.6 ft was 1.72 (plate-appearance estimand) and 1.49 (pitch estimand) times the true value of its chosen spots (-52.5 and -45.7 against -30.6); held on side and band pricing for the plate-appearance estimand (1.23 and 1.24), failed for the pitch estimand (1.07 and 1.06, so with the pitch estimand the repricing removes most of the overstatement when the maps are good).** In small worlds (nearer the real spreads) the ratios were 2.03 and 1.63 for points, 1.48 and 1.19 by side, 1.46 and 1.11 by band. (4) held on size (one-standard-deviation values 78 to 104% of the truth in target, opposing and coincident); failed on the placebo's interval for the pitch estimand, which excluded zero in 2, 3 and 3 of 10 worlds (4 of 10 in effect) with means within 0.00002 of zero: the game bootstrap of one random reassignment is too narrow for the pitch estimand, so the placebo check must read the spread across reassignments (already reported as outside_more_reassignments) rather than that interval; the plate-appearance placebo 1, 0 and 2 of 10. (5) held: noisy one-standard-deviation value 83% of the truth, below effect's, excluding zero in 10 of 10. Level worlds (hitters differ only in how much they swing): the within-hitter coefficient read zero (-0.00002), correctly finding no location structure, while the own-map coefficients read -0.00055 and -0.00038 and the points figure excluded zero in 4 and 7 of 10 (the own part carries a swing level that also moves misses). **Under the rules set in advance: the own-map figures in VALUE-08F, VALUE-11, VALUE-15 and VALUE-16 carry a false-positive note (their primary estimator, within hitter and neighbor-held, passed); the stated 32 runs is the regression's figure, overstating the true value of its choices by 1.3 to 2.0 in these worlds, and is replaced by VALUE-18F's structural figure when scored.** The regression keeps its place as the check on the structural pricing (its per-point value is unbiased to within 15% on the one-standard-deviation scale in every world with location structure). | held in the main: regression figure overstates 1.3 to 2.0x, own-map intervals too narrow for the plate-appearance estimand |
| VALUE-18 | Structural pricing of the aiming edge (the fix VALUE-17's smoke results called for). Instead of one regression coefficient times the fitted gain, each scattered aim is priced by the engine's own components: the hitter's own swing part at that point, scaled by its out-of-sample calibration (the test season's swing residual regressed on the own part in probability units, by side of the zone; near 1 when the maps are real, near 0 when they are noise), times the value if he swings minus the value if he takes, built from the whiff model with his whiff map, the called-strike, foul and ball-in-play value models with his and the pitcher's terms (PAModels, the planner's fitted pieces) and the training seasons' count values. The pitcher's best third is chosen by that structural value, so the choice weighs where an extra swing is expensive, not only where the hitter swings most. Interval: hitters resampled and the calibration slope resampled by game. No regression coefficient enters; the regression stays beside it as the check. Smoke results before registration (60,000-plate-appearance worlds, local, disclosed): effect world at 0.6 ft, structural -41.6 against the true value of its own choices -41.5 (points -41.2 against -21.9), at zero scatter -69.1 against -59.8; the structural choices were worth more than the points choices (-41.5 against -21.9; oracle -54.1); small world (40% effects) -11.1 against -12.2 (points -7.4 against -6.2; bands -7.6 against -4.0), calibration 0.56; null world -0.1 with the calibration slope 0.0002 [-0.11, +0.14] and the interval covering zero; the engine's structural per-point value correlated 0.93 with the generator's at the test pitches | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the lane runs (ten worlds per kind, seeds 100-109, the structural option on; and one development run on the real 2025 test season with maps from 2023-2024).** Predictions, synthetic: (1) null: the structural figure's interval covers zero in at least nine of ten worlds and the calibration slope's interval covers zero in at least nine; (2) effect, small, target, opposing, coincident: the structural figure at 0.6 ft is within 20% of the true value of its own choices on average (ratio 0.8 to 1.2) in each kind; (3) the structural choice's true value beats the points choice's in at least eight of ten worlds in each of those kinds; (4) noisy: the calibration slope below effect's, the ratio within 0.7 to 1.3. Real data (development, descriptive, no prediction registered on its size): the 2025 structural figure, its calibration slopes, and the regression's figure beside it. Decision rule: if (1) and (2) hold, VALUE-18F is registered: the structural pricing scored once, frozen, on the untouched months (maps from 2023-2025), and its figure replaces the regression's 32 runs as the stated value of the aiming edge, the regression's figure kept as the check; if (2) fails, the structural figure is not stated and VALUE-17's overstatement factor is written beside the 32 runs; if (1) fails, the calibration does not protect against noise maps and the pricing is set aside. Receipts: research/discovery-value_synth-<run>.json (one per kind) and research/discovery-value2-<run>.json. Development result, real 2025 (run 37964377494; 617,627 test pitches; maps 2023-2024; pitch estimand; menus from the pitcher's 2023-2024 spots): the own part's calibration on 2025 swings 0.954 [0.932, 0.978] outside and 0.820 [0.797, 0.848] inside (the maps are real: a fitted point is 0.95 of a real point); the regression's per-point value -0.000731 [-0.000817, -0.000658] (pitch estimand; the plate-appearance estimand gave -0.000553), placebo +0.000053 [-0.000034, +0.000119]; by distance band, 0 to 1 inch outside -0.000168 (nil), 1 to 2 -0.000422, 2 to 4 -0.000576, over 4 -0.001055, deep inside +0.000969; aiming at 0.6 ft, outside only, per 6,200: **points -25.7 [-28.7, -23.1], by side -21.5, by band -25.9, structural -43.3 [-45.6, -41.1]** (inside, structural -21.7); 476 hitters priced. The structural choice spends its gain farther off the plate (1.70 of the 2.93 points beyond 4 inches). No figure is stated from this development run; the synthetic verdict decides. Synthetic results (runs 37964411619 null, 37964455287 level, 37964498333 effect, 37964538154 small, 37964584205 target, 37964625900 opposing, 37964669223 coincident, 37964714378 noisy; ten 200,000-plate-appearance worlds each, 230,000 to 250,000 test pitches per world, pitch estimand, 0.6 ft): **(1) held: in the null worlds the structural figure read -0.4 runs per 6,200 with its interval covering zero in 10 of 10 and the calibration slope 0.03 with its interval covering zero in 10 of 10** (the points figure +1.3, excluding zero in 1 of 10; the band-priced figure -2.8, excluding zero in 6 of 10, so the band regression's interval is too narrow under the null). **(2) held: the structural figure against the true value of its own choices, effect -56.7 against -54.2 (ratio 1.05, per world 1.03 to 1.06), small -18.7 against -19.1 (0.98; 0.93 to 1.05), target -72.1 against -75.4 (0.96; 0.92 to 1.00), opposing -86.8 against -86.6 (1.00; 0.99 to 1.02), coincident -56.5 against -54.0 (1.05; 0.99 to 1.10)**, its interval covering the truth in 10, 10, 8, 10 and 9 of 10; the points figure in the same worlds 1.49, 1.63, 1.32, 1.36 and 1.52 times the truth of its choices, covering it in 0 to 4 of 10. **(3) held: the structural choice was worth more than the points choice in 10 of 10 worlds of every kind (effect -54.2 against -30.6; small -19.1 against -10.5; target -75.4 against -54.1; opposing -86.6 against -66.8; coincident -54.0 against -30.6; the oracle -64, -25, -88, -100, -64)**. (4) held in part: noisy, ratio 0.95 (0.92 to 0.97) within the band, choices better in 10 of 10, but the calibration slope came out above effect's (1.17 against 1.07: maps fitted on fewer pitches are shrunk more, so a fitted point is more than a real point; the prediction had the direction wrong) and the interval covered the truth in only 2 of 10 (it resamples hitters and the calibration, not the maps' own noise). Level worlds (no location-specific structure): structural -0.5 against a true -0.2, excluding zero in 0 of 10, where the points figure read -4.7 and excluded zero in 7 of 10. The engine's structural per-point value correlated 0.94 to 0.96 with the generator's at the test pitches in every kind; calibration slopes 1.07 (effect), 0.74 (small), 0.97, 1.03, 1.07. **Under the rule, VALUE-18F is registered below; the structural figure becomes the stated value of the aiming edge once scored there, the regression's figure kept as the check.** Code change recorded: the Report tab's aim plans are chosen and priced structurally from this commit (tools/brl_report.py, STRUCTURAL = True, calibration constants 0.95 and 0.82 from the development run until VALUE-18F's), each spot labeled by where its value comes from (his extra chases outside, or the strikes he takes when the pitch runs in). | held: structural within 5% of the truth of its choices in five kinds, nil in null; choices worth 1.3 to 1.8 times the points choices |
| VALUE-18F | The structural pricing of the aiming edge scored once on the later-period months (2026 from August 1 through September 27, regular season; rule 6: looked at before, reused validation, not untouched), maps and the league from 2023-2025, the pitch estimand's regression beside it as the check, menus from the pitcher's 2023-2025 spots, 0.6 ft of scatter, outside only as the stated figure (inside beside it). value2_study with final_eval, frozen at the commit named in the lane's parameters | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: (1) the structural outside-only figure between -30 and -60 runs per 6,200 plate appearances (development 2025 gave -43.3; the regression's figures on these months were -32 with the plate-appearance estimand); (2) the own part's calibration slope outside between 0.85 and 1.05; (3) the pitch-estimand regression figure (points, 0.6 ft) between -20 and -40, and its placebo's spread across reassignments covering zero. Decision rule: the structural figure with its interval is the stated value of the aiming edge on the public pages and in METHODS.md, described as estimated policy potential on the later-period months, with the regression's figure beside it; if (1) fails low (under 30), the stated figure is the structural one all the same and the 32 runs is withdrawn as overstated; if (2) fails, the calibration constants in the Report tool are replaced by the measured ones. Forward confirmation remains the 2026 postseason and 2027. Receipt: research/discovery-value2-<run>.json Results (run 37975280294; 184,318 test pitches, 412 hitters priced, menus from 2023-2025): **structural outside-only -43.5 runs per 6,200 plate appearances [-46.6, -40.7] (hitters and the calibration slope resampled), inside -23.3**; calibration slope 0.960 [0.921, 1.001] outside and 0.830 inside; the regression's figures on the same months and choices, pitch estimand: points -29.4 [-34.0, -25.0], by side -24.4, by band -27.9; own-part coefficient -0.000847 [-0.000980, -0.000720] per fitted point, within hitter and neighbor-held -0.000776; placebo +0.000131 with its game interval excluding zero but its spread across five more reassignments (-0.000013 to +0.000194) covering it, as VALUE-17 said to read it. (1), (2) and (3) held. **One disagreement to carry: at the test pitches the engine's structural per-point value averages -0.00116 (implied regression coefficient -0.00117) against the realized-outcome regression's -0.00085, so the structure prices an extra swing 38% higher than the outcomes of these months show; the synthetic worlds could not test this because there the structure was true by construction.** Under the rule the stated value of the aiming edge is the structural figure with the regression beside it, written as a range: **about 30 to 45 runs over a team's season from outside pitches alone (43.5 by the engine's components, 29 by realized outcomes), estimated policy potential on the later-period months**, with the inside part (about 20) unstated as before. The Report tool's calibration constants stay 0.95 and 0.82 (measured 0.96 and 0.83). | held: structural -43.5 [-46.6, -40.7]; stated as 30 to 45 with the outcome regression beside it |
| VALUE-19 | Is the engine's structure calibrated against what happened? VALUE-18F's structural per-point value ran 38% above the realized-outcome regression's on the later-period months. Check (diagnostic, descriptive): on the test pitches, the realized telescoping value regressed on the structure's expected value of the same pitch (the hitter's swing chance times the swing value plus the take chance times the take value, each relative to the current count), overall, outside and inside the zone; the swing value against realized values on actual swings and the take value on actual takes, with their mean gaps outside the zone; the placebo's interval now read across reassignments. Development 2025 (maps 2023-2024) and the later-period months (maps 2023-2025). Synthetic reference (effect world, where the structure is true by construction): slope 0.94 overall, 0.90 outside, 0.86 for the swing value on swings, 1.01 for the take value on takes | Reading set in advance (no numeric prediction; diagnostic): a slope near the synthetic reference means the structure prices pitches as outcomes do and the 30 to 45 range stands; a slope well below it (under 0.75 outside) means the structure is overconfident and VALUE-20 registers an outcome-calibrated structural pricing (the structural value scaled by the measured slope) before the stated range changes. Receipts: research/discovery-value2-<run>.json Results (runs 37977331791 development 2025, 617,627 test pitches; 37977374753 later-period months, 184,318): **the structure is calibrated against outcomes: realized value on the structure's expected value, slope 0.95 and 0.99 overall, 0.93 and 0.94 outside, 0.98 and 1.03 inside; the swing value on actual swings 1.09 and 1.07, the take value on actual takes 0.99 and 1.02** (synthetic reference 0.94, 0.90, 0.86, 1.01). Mean gaps outside the zone: the structure's swing value sits 0.0019 and 0.0030 runs above realized swings and its take value 0.0021 and 0.0046 below realized takes, so the realized swing-against-take difference on outside pitches is 3 to 6% larger than the structure's (-0.116), not smaller. The per-point gap (structure -0.00116 and -0.00116 against the regression's -0.00073 and -0.00085) is therefore not a miscalibrated structure; the regression's single coefficient averages over pitches where an extra swing costs more or less than at a hitter's own chase spots, and the synthetic worlds show regression pricing of a chosen set is unreliable in both directions (it priced the points choice 1.5 times too high and, by band, the structural choice at 0.46 of its true value in the effect world). The placebo read across reassignments: [-0.00008, +0.00008] and [-0.00005, +0.00019], both covering zero. **Reading under the rule: the 30 to 45 range stands, with the structural figure the better estimate of the structural choice's value and the regression's figure the better-anchored floor.** | diagnostic: structure calibrated (slopes 0.93 to 1.03); the range stands |
