| PLAN-02S | The planner priced by the generator (synthetic worlds only; diagnostic, no real data): PLAN-01's machinery (PAModels fitted on the world's 2023-2024, pools of each pitcher's pitches, value iteration over counts and expectation states) run on 60,000-plate-appearance worlds, 40 pairs each, and every policy's true value computed with the generator's own probabilities (tools/brl_synth.py: true_probs_for_pool, policy_value, planner_truth; scratch synth/plan_truth.py and plan_truth2.py): the pitcher's actual mix, the league plan, the hitter plan, the oracle; the planner's own claim beside the truth; then the policy softened to a uniform choice among the best fraction of the pool in each state, and the hitter's maps scaled by the out-of-sample calibration slope VALUE-18 measures (1.07 effect, 0.74 small, 0.03 null) | **The single-best-pitch planner's hitter-specific claim does not track the truth at realistic sizes: effect world true +41.8 runs per 6,100 plate appearances over the league plan (claimed +37.3), small world true -8.2 (claimed -0.4), null world true -10.1 (claimed +14.8); following noise maps costs runs while the planner believes it gains.** Softening alone (best 10% of the pool) helps where effects are large (effect +57.7 true, 90% of pairs) but not elsewhere (small +0.7 true against +12.6 claimed; null -11.4 true against +12.4 claimed). **Calibrated maps with the soft policy track the truth in all three worlds: effect true +59.0, claimed +40.6; small true +5.1, claimed +4.6; null true +0.2, claimed -0.1.** The league plan's value over the actual mix (0.21 runs per plate appearance in every world) is the planner exploiting hitters who never adapt and is not a claim about anything real; the same figure in PLAN-01's real-data run (114.9 per 6,100) is read the same way. Reading: the planner stays set aside for the product; PLAN-03, when taken up, is the calibrated soft planner (own part scaled by its measured calibration, the best tenth of the pool per state) with a sequential off-policy evaluation checked first in these worlds, where a plan's true value is known. The hitter-specific increment of a plan over the league plan at realistic map sizes is small here (about 5 runs per 6,100) beside the structural aiming edge, which is a different quantity (the best third of outside spots against the pitcher's own mix, hitter-specific part only). A first off-policy check (scratch synth/ope_synth.py; logged 2025 pitches reweighted by the target policy's share of the pitch's family-by-zone bin over the pitcher's share, 150 pairs, 2,860 pitches, median effective sample size 6 per pair) read a one-step increment of 11 runs per 6,100 against a true 64 and the model's 58: the planner's gains come from placement inside a bin (the exact corner among the pitcher's pitches) that logged data cannot resolve and that command scatter would erase, so **PLAN-03's actions must be aims under command scatter, the policy a landing cloud over bins, and the planner solved on scatter-averaged transitions**, which also keeps it from exploiting corners no pitcher can hit on purpose. A second cut with aims under 0.6 ft of scatter (scratch synth/ope_scatter.py; the policy's landing cloud over 75 family-by-location bins against the pitcher's landing shares, 120 pairs, 2,320 logged pitches): the off-policy levels of the league-aim and hitter-aim policies now track the truth (-0.0218 against -0.0229 and -0.0265 against -0.0236 runs per pitch), but the hitter-specific increment is small (true 0.0007 runs per pitch) and the estimate's noise at this size is several times that; a real-data version needs the full season's pairs and should state the increment with its interval and abstain below an effective sample size. | diagnostic: argmin planner misreads noise as gain; calibrated soft planner tracks the truth; actions must be aims under scatter; the increment is small |
| TOTALS-04 | Where the totals distribution goes wrong (the audit's priority 3; diagnostic, descriptive, development replays only: PROD-03's v2-prod3-1000 replays, 2,423 games of 2025 and 2,427 of 2026; scratch harness/totals_diag.py): combined and team-level PIT coverage, tails, CRPS and log score; predictive against residual variance; first five innings against the rest and the inning profile; home-away residual dependence; extras; subsets by day or night, roof, temperature, wind speed and direction (public game conditions); park bias; and a negative-binomial challenger on the simulator's own team mean (mean a + b times the simulator's, dispersion fitted by maximum likelihood on one season, scored on the other) against the simulator's histogram and a Poisson | **Means are right everywhere they were checked**: combined total bias -0.03 runs in both seasons, home -0.06 and -0.05, away +0.03 and +0.02; first five innings -0.03 and -0.01 against after the fifth +0.00 and -0.02 (the inning profile matches within 0.04 runs per team-inning through the seventh); the home-away residual correlation is 0.00 and -0.01, so the independence the convolution assumes holds. **The shape is slightly narrow**: predictive variance of a team's runs 9.2 against a residual variance of 10.0 to 10.1 (8 to 10% short); middle-half coverage 0.48 against 0.50 and 10.4 to 11% in each tail against 10%. **The negative-binomial challenger on the simulator's own mean (dispersion 3.4 to 3.6, slope 0.98 and 0.84, intercept 0.12 and 0.73) matches the histogram on CRPS (1.7143 against 1.7145 and 1.7250 against 1.7238 per team-game; combined 2.4643 against 2.4659 and 2.4856 against 2.4862) but beats it on the log score by 0.047 nats per team-game (2.452 against 2.499; 2.455 against 2.503) with coverage 0.497 and tails 8 to 10%**; a Poisson on the same mean is far worse (coverage 0.34 to 0.36, tails 17 to 21%). Reading: the simulator's mean carries the information; its 1,000-world histograms carry Monte Carlo noise in the bins (a log-score cost, not a CRPS one) and a little too little spread. Subsets: no condition shifts the mean consistently across both seasons beyond noise except wind of 12 mph or more (-0.24 and -0.22, the simulator low); park biases have a standard deviation of 0.59 and 0.64 runs across parks against about 0.5 expected from noise alone, with Colorado -1.25 (2025) and Sacramento -1.53 (2026) at the low end (the simulator under those parks) and Kansas City +1.62 (2025) and Los Angeles Angels +1.07 (2026) at the high end. Extras (8.6% of games): the simulator's mean is 0.57 and 0.93 runs under those games' totals, as a pregame number must be. Decisions: (1) the published run distributions should be smoothed (TOTALS-05: a negative-binomial fit with the simulator's mean and its own variance inflated to the residual variance, or more worlds; registered before any change to the page); (2) a park adjustment is the one feature with a visible target (TOTALS-06, registered with a prediction before its run); (3) the page keeps stating no over chance of its own (TOTALS-03). | diagnostic: means right, shape 8-10% narrow, histogram bins noisy, park extremes missed |
| REPORT-01 | The matchup report (product, Alec's direction October 9: coaches first, past games evaluable with information up to each game): tools/brl_report.py builds one document per game (public/reports/<date>/<pk>.json on the data branch; the site's Report tab reads it) from the credited pitch models fitted on pitches before the report's as-of date: the decision-moment swing representation with the league's family part and hitter family maps (MATCHUP-01F, MATCHUP-05F), whiff maps on the true crossing (MATCHUP-03), batter zones (MATCHUP-08F), the count chain at ENGINE-01's calibration, and the aim plan with VALUE-08F's run value. For finished games a grade: outside pitches in the recommended cells against the pitcher's usual share, the hitters' chases against the league's and their maps' expectations, and the plate appearance results. Scheduled twice daily (9:20 and 16:35 Eastern; yesterday graded, today with posted lineups when available); the 2026 season backfilled month by month with the maps as of the first of each month (never a pitch from the game or later). | Product. First documents October 9, 2026: the October 7 and 8 postseason games (e.g. 849832: 26 of 144 outside pitches in the recommended cells against 29 at the staffs' usual rates; 33 chases against 37.9 league and 36.5 map expectation). The grades accumulate into a forward record of the maps' calibration and of staffs' aiming (EXPLOIT-01's finding, now daily); a season of grades is scored under its own registration before any claim. | product; forward record from October 9 |
| SEQ-01 | Matchup program M4, how unexpected a pitch is. What a hitter can know: how often this pitcher throws each pitch type in this count bucket (first pitch, ahead, even, behind, full), after this previous pitch in the plate appearance, to this side, on earlier days, shrunk toward the league in the same context (70 contexts). Surprise = -log of the probability of the type actually thrown. Feed pitches 2023-2025; gradient boosting with and without surprise, the context's entropy and the six type probabilities, on top of speed, movement, location, spin, release, extension, count, type, platoon, the hitter's swing habit and the previous type; trained on 2023-2024, scored on 2025, paired by game, for swings, whiffs on swings and called strikes on takes. Checked on synthetic data first: a planted whiff effect gains 4.6 nats per 1,000 swings; with none the extra features cost 0.15 to 0.31 (so a real effect must clear that) | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions on 2025: whiffs on swings gain at least 0.5 nats per 1,000 swings with the interval excluding zero, and the observed whiff rate rises across surprise quintiles beyond the model without it; called strikes on takes gain at least 0.5 per 1,000 takes (hitters take what they did not expect); swing decisions gain less than whiffs. Decision rule: if the whiff gain holds, surprise enters the pitch-level matchup engine (its pitch-selection part is this same usage model) and a pitcher-level predictability cost is computed for scouting; if no gain clears 0.5, the hitter's expectation by count and sequence carries nothing beyond the pitch itself at this resolution. Results (run 37876755436; 2025: 705,553 pitches, 337,416 swings, 368,137 takes): surprise averages 1.93 bits (SD 0.91) against a context entropy of 2.06 bits. Gains in nats per 1,000: swings -0.29 [-0.43, -0.16], whiffs on swings -0.44 [-0.65, -0.23], called strikes on takes -0.88 [-1.03, -0.72], all at or below the cost of the extra features in the null synthetic world. Across surprise quintiles (0.9 to 3.4 bits) the observed whiff rate (21.5 to 23.5%) follows the model without surprise (22.2 to 23.5%) within half a point. **Every prediction failed: how unexpected a pitch type is, given the pitcher's habits in that count, after that pitch, to that side, adds nothing to swings, whiffs or called strikes beyond the pitch's own physics and location.** Ruled out at this resolution: the hitter's count-and-sequence expectation of the pitch type as a source of whiffs (consistent with pitchers mixing well enough to leave nothing to exploit, or with hitters not acting on type expectations); together with tunneling's weak result (DISC), deception has to be sought in the flight at the decision moment, not in the choice of pitch. | no gain; ruled out |
| EXPOSURE-01 | What the times-through-the-order penalty is, if not velocity (FATIGUE-01) or reading the flight better (Decision Horizon): familiarity with this pitcher's pitch types. For each pitch: how many pitches of the same type this hitter has already seen from this pitcher in the game, how many of any type (total exposure), and how many of the same type from other pitchers in the game (control: learning one pitcher's pitch should not transfer fully). Feed pitches 2023-2025, gradient boosting trained on 2023-2024 and scored on 2025, paired by game: whiffs on swings, chases, launch speed on balls in play, beyond the pitch's physics, location, count, the pitcher's pitch count, times through the order, total exposure and the hitter's swing habit. Checked on synthetic games first: a planted learning effect gains 1.07 nats per 1,000 swings (three more same-type pitches: -3.4 points of whiff rate); with none, -0.34 and 0.0 | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions on 2025: three more same-type pitches from this pitcher lower the whiff rate on swings by 0.5 to 2.0 points, with a gain of at least 0.5 nats per 1,000 swings (the interval excluding zero); the same exposure from other pitchers moves it less than half as much; chases fall 0.3 to 1.5 points per three; launch speed on contact rises 0.2 to 1.0 mph per three. Decision rule: if the whiff and chase effects hold, pitch-type familiarity replaces the fixed times-through-the-order term in the matchup engine (pitch-mix order then matters, and a scouting view shows which pitches a starter has already shown each hitter); if not, familiarity at the pitch-type level is ruled out as the penalty's source. Results (run 37877474263; 2025: 336,846 swings, 347,997 pitches outside the zone, 124,100 balls in play): whiffs on swings fall steeply with same-type exposure (25.3% at none, 20.2% at three, 15.9% at seven) but the model without it predicts nearly the same (25.5, 20.8, 17.7: total exposure, times through the order and pitch count carry it); gain -0.06 nats per 1,000 swings [-0.23, +0.11]; three more same-type pitches from this pitcher -0.74 points, from other pitchers -0.63 (not specific to the pitcher). Chases: gain -0.08 [-0.26, +0.10], three more same-type pitches +0.96 points (the wrong direction). Launch speed on contact: +0.20 mph per three [mean squared error down 0.19, 0.10 to 0.27]. Predictions held only for the size of the whiff effect and, at its lower edge, launch speed; the gain, the pitcher-specific contrast and the chase direction failed. **Under the rule, pitch-type familiarity with the pitcher is ruled out as the source of the times-through-the-order penalty**: hitters do miss less as a game goes on, but seeing the same pitch type from any pitcher counts as much as seeing it from him, and none of it adds to total exposure. With FATIGUE-01 (velocity, 1.4% of the penalty) and the Decision Horizon (no better reading of the flight), three explanations are now out; remaining candidates are command that degrades without speed loss and timing that adapts to the game in general. | ruled out as TTO source |
| CONTACT-02 | Which part of a hitter's swing geometry carries the miss information CONTACT-01 found, and is it more than training noise? Same data and split as CONTACT-01 (Savant: train 2025, score 2026 through July). (1) The flexible comparisons refitted three times on bootstrap resamples of training games with feature subsampling (the game bootstrap on the test set does not see how much a boosted model's gain moves when it is refitted; a synthetic check with two pure-noise hitter features showed +0.66 nats per 1,000 at a smaller sample). (2) Nine parametric terms on the rates model's cross-fitted logits, all together, each left out and each alone: timing spread alone, with speed, and with the late change in approach angle (the plane at the steering limit, 110 ms, against the plane at the plate); usual attack angle with height, with approach angle, with vertical movement; the plane formula F; the hitter's attack-angle spread alone and with height. (3) The flexible model with more geometry (attack-angle spread, swing path tilt, attack direction) | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: the geometry gain over the rates model averages at least 0.5 nats per 1,000 swings across refits and is positive in every refit; the parametric terms together recover at least half of it; the two largest drop-one losses are attack angle with height and timing spread with the late plane change; the extra geometry adds at least 0.2 on average. Decision rule: if the gain survives the refits, the parametric terms that carry it (drop-one loss at least 0.05 each) form a frozen swing-geometry miss model scored once on the untouched set; if it does not survive, CONTACT-01's geometry gain is recorded as within training noise. Results (run 37879352713; train 309,477 swings, test 225,466): the geometry gain over the rates model in three refits on resampled training games: 0.39, 0.66, 0.55 nats per 1,000 swings (mean 0.53, every refit positive; the single fit's 1.04 sat at the high end); the extra geometry (attack-angle spread, path tilt, attack direction) adds 0.26, 0.59, 0.92 (mean 0.59). The nine parametric terms together gain 0.19 [0.07, 0.32], 18% of the single fit's flexible gain. Drop-one losses: timing spread 0.169, attack angle with approach angle 0.013, F 0.013, timing spread with speed 0.012, the rest at or below 0.006 (attack angle with height -0.022). Alone: timing spread 0.22 (coefficient -0.90: hitters with a wider spread of contact depth miss less, beyond their whiff rate), attack angle with height 0.07, F 0.11, attack angle with approach angle 0.04. Predictions held for the refit mean and sign and for the extra geometry; failed for the parametric share (18% against at least half) and for which terms carry it. **Under the rule the gain survives refitting, and the frozen miss model is the rates model plus the one term with a drop-one loss of at least 0.05, the hitter's contact-depth spread; it is scored once on the untouched set (CONTACT-03).** Reading, to be tested: the spread of contact depth is not timing error as the M1 equation assumed. Contact depth is only seen when contact is made, so a hitter who still meets the ball when early or late shows a wide spread and one who misses when off time shows a narrow one: it measures his contact window, and a wide window means fewer misses. If so, hitters with wide windows should lose less to changes of speed (an offspeed pitch after fastballs), a matchup-specific prediction for the next registration. | gain survives refits (0.53); timing spread carries it, sign reversed |
| TIMING-01 | How much of the previous pitch's timing a hitter carries into his next swing, measured where contact happens. A hitter times his swing to the flight he expects; if a share alpha of that expectation is carried from the previous pitch of the plate appearance, a pitch whose flight from release to the front of the plate is longer by dt finds him early by alpha times dt, and contact is made farther in front of him by u times alpha times dt (u = v_s v_b / (v_s + v_b), the speed at which the meeting point moves along the flight per unit of timing error, about 56 ft/s or 6.7 inches per 10 ms at 70 mph bat and 85 mph ball; the M1 equation wrote the denominator as a difference, the numbers there used the sum). Savant contact swings (contact depth: intercept, inches toward the pitcher) with a previous pitch in the same plate appearance, the hitter's usual depth (earlier days, shrunk) removed, against dt with the pitch's own flight time, type, side and height, count and platoon held fixed, fitted on 2025 and repeated on 2026 through July: the slope over 6.7 inches per 10 ms is the carryover share. Also: after swings against after takes, two pitches back beyond the previous one, each hitter's own carryover (shrunk slope; its correlation between the first and second halves of 2025), and misses on speed changes: on top of a flexible whiff model that already sees the speed change (flight time, dt, its size, the previous pitch's type and whether he swung, the hitter's whiff rate), each hitter's carryover and contact window (CONTACT-02) with their products with the size of the speed change, coefficients fitted on 2025 and scored on 2026 through July. Checked on synthetic plate appearances first: with a planted carryover share of 0.25 (between-hitter SD 0.12) the depth slope is 1.56 inches per 10 ms (share 0.23), hitters' own carryover correlates 0.96 between halves, and misses on speed changes gain 1.25 nats per 1,000 swings [0.77, 1.73] with carryover times speed change +0.032 [0.028, 0.039]; with none, the slope is 0.002 [-0.012, 0.017], the halves correlate 0.05 and nothing gains | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: (1) the depth slope positive with the interval excluding zero in 2025 and in 2026, a carryover share between 0.03 and 0.30; (2) two pitches back adds less than half the previous pitch's slope; (3) after a swing against after a take: no direction predicted, reported; (4) each hitter's carryover correlates at least 0.2 between the halves of 2025; (5) misses: carryover times speed change positive with the interval excluding zero, and its gain positive with the interval excluding zero on 2026; (6) contact window times speed change negative (a wide window blunts a speed change) with the interval excluding zero. Decision rule: (1) holding makes timing carryover a measured relationship of the program (the share of the previous pitch's timing in the next swing); (4) and (5) together make each hitter's carryover a matchup input (pitchers whose speeds differ most against hitters who carry most) and a scouting number, frozen and scored once on the untouched set before it is used; if (1) fails, hitters re-time every pitch, and speed sequencing does not act through carried timing. Results (run 37882064459; contact swings with a previous pitch: 197,598 in 2025, 141,042 in 2026 through July; speed change SD 37 ms): depth slope **+0.038 inches per 10 ms [0.021, 0.051] (2025) and +0.043 [0.027, 0.060] (2026)**, a carryover share of 0.006, ten times below the predicted range. **After a take the slope is zero (+0.002 [-0.015, +0.016]; 2026 +0.012); after a swing it is +0.11 more [0.091, 0.129] (2026 +0.095 [0.073, 0.115])**: timing carries over only from a pitch the hitter swung at, a share of about 1.7%. Two pitches back (pitch three on): the previous pitch's own coefficient nearly vanishes (-0.02 per 10 ms of its flight time) while the pitch two back carries -0.16, the opposite of a decaying memory. Each hitter's slope: between-hitter SD 0.16 inches per 10 ms, four times the pooled mean, **correlation between the halves of 2025: 0.60**. Misses (263,153 training swings, 171,651 test): the hitter's slope as a main term -0.51 [-0.62, -0.43] (hitters whose contact moves out front on slower pitches miss less overall) and times the size of the speed change **+0.055 [0.028, 0.078]**, gain 0.17 nats per 1,000 swings [0.09, 0.28]; contact window main term -0.91 (as in CONTACT-02) but times the speed change +0.010 [-0.049, +0.054]. Predictions: (1) held for the sign in both seasons, failed for the size; (2) failed; (3) carryover after swings only; (4) held (0.60); (5) held; (6) failed (the window does not blunt speed changes). Reading, before any use: the after-swing contrast is the cleanest result (the same speed change, after a swing against after a take); the pooled and per-hitter slopes are open to an artifact, because the pitch's own flight time enters linearly with its type, so any curvature in how depth follows a pitch's own speed is picked up by the speed change and by the pitch two back (pitchers alternate types, so the pitch two back resembles the current one). The per-hitter number may be how a hitter's contact depth follows pitch speed in general (an adjustability trait) rather than carried timing. **Under the rule (4) and (5) make the hitter's slope a matchup input to be frozen and scored on the untouched set; the freeze waits for TIMING-02, which removes the artifact (within each pitcher's pitch type) and separates the two traits, so the one untouched scoring is spent on a measure whose meaning is known.** | after-swing carryover +0.11 in/10 ms; hitter slope reliable (0.60); artifact check next |
| TIMING-02 | TIMING-01 without its artifact, and which hitter trait it found. (a) Carried timing measured within one pitcher's one pitch type in one season (fixed effects, flight times centered there): the same pitch preceded by different pitches; contact depth (hitter's usual depth removed) on the previous pitch's flight time after a take, after a foul and after a miss, and on the pitch two back. (b) Speed-follow: how far contact moves out front per 10 ms of slower flight across one pitcher's pitches, pooled (6.7 inches would be no re-timing at all, 0 perfect re-timing) and per hitter (shrunk slope, 2025), against the carried trait (each hitter's after-swing slope within pitcher-types); reliability across the halves of 2025. (c) Misses in 2026 through July on top of a flexible whiff model that sees flight time, slowness against the pitcher's average, the speed change and whether he swung at the previous pitch: speed-follow with its products with slowness and with the speed change; carried with its product with the speed change after a swing. Checked on synthetic plate appearances first, in three worlds: a pitcher-level depth effect with no carried timing gives TIMING-01's estimator a false slope (-0.054 [-0.067, -0.040]) and this one none (after a take -0.001, after a swing -0.009, both intervals across zero); planted carried timing after swings (share 0.15) and hitter speed-follow (SD 0.3 inches per 10 ms) are recovered as an after-take slope of -0.002, an after-swing slope of -0.58 (share 0.087: contact is only seen when the timing error was small enough, so the estimate runs low), speed-follow reliability 0.59 against 0.30 for carried, and speed-follow times slowness +0.59 on misses; with neither, everything is near zero (speed-follow reliability 0.05) | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: (1) within pitcher-types, after a take the previous pitch's flight time moves contact depth by at most 0.03 inches per 10 ms either way, in both seasons; after a swing it moves it negatively (a slower previous pitch, a later swing) with the interval excluding zero, a carried share between 0.005 and 0.05, in both seasons; (2) two pitches back after a swing, less than half of that; (3) pooled speed-follow between 0.3 and 1.2 inches per 10 ms (4 to 18% of a speed difference not re-timed), interval excluding zero; (4) hitters' speed-follow correlates at least 0.4 between the halves of 2025, more than the carried trait; (5) on 2026 misses, speed-follow times slowness positive with the interval excluding zero, and the speed-follow terms gain with the interval excluding zero; (6) the carried trait adds under 0.05 nats per 1,000 swings beyond speed-follow. Decision rule: (1) makes timing carried from a swung-at pitch, and only from one, a measured mechanism with its share; (4) and (5) make speed-follow the hitter timing measure of the matchup engine in place of TIMING-01's slope, frozen and scored once on the untouched set (TIMING-03) before it is used or shown; if (5) fails, speed-follow describes hitters but does not predict their misses. Results (run 37882811823; within 3,672 pitcher-types in 2025 and 3,375 in 2026; 197,410 and 140,872 contact swings): the pitch's own flight within its type +1.23 inches per 10 ms (2025) and +1.16 (2026). **After a take, a slower previous pitch puts contact farther out front: +0.106 inches per 10 ms of the previous pitch's flight [0.094, 0.120] (2025), +0.098 [0.081, 0.114] (2026)**, the opposite of carried timing. After a swing the previous pitch's slope drops by 0.081 [0.054, 0.110] (2026: 0.051), after a miss by 0.134 more (2026: 0.205), so a missed pitch's timing does carry (net -0.11 and -0.16 inches per 10 ms, shares 0.016 and 0.023) and a fouled one nets about zero. Two pitches back: after a take -0.015 [-0.031, +0.001], after a swing -0.18 more [-0.21, -0.15] (2026 -0.17): a swing two pitches back still carries. Speed-follow within each pitcher, across his pitch types: **+1.53 inches per 10 ms [1.49, 1.57] in 2025 and +1.52 in 2026: 23% of a flight-time difference between one pitcher's pitches is not re-timed.** Hitters' speed-follow: SD 0.34 inches per 10 ms, halves correlate 0.54; the carried trait: SD 0.11, halves 0.18. Misses on 2026 swings: speed-follow -0.21 [-0.27, -0.17] (hitters whose contact moves out front on slower pitches miss less), times slowness +0.007 [-0.005, +0.019], times the speed change +0.021 [0.008, 0.035], gain 0.17 [0.08, 0.27]; the carried trait adds 0.002. Predictions (1) failed (takes move timing, in the opposite direction), (2) failed (two back carries more than one back), (3) failed (1.53, above the range), (4) held, (5) failed for the slowness product (the gain held), (6) held. **Under the rule nothing is promoted: carried timing is not a single mechanism, and speed-follow does not pass (5) as registered.** What the run found instead, to be confirmed with fuller controls before any claim: two opposite sequence effects in where contact is made: after a taken pitch, hitters meet the next pitch as if expecting the other speed (a slow pitch taken, the next one met earlier: pitchers alternate speeds and hitters appear to know it), and the timing of a swing, above all a miss, persists for one or two pitches. The speed-follow trait predicts misses through its main term, which may overlap the contact window (CONTACT-03); TIMING-03 tests both. | contrast after takes +0.10 in/10 ms; nothing promoted |
| TIMING-03 | TIMING-02's two sequence effects with fuller controls (development data: Savant 2025 and 2026 through July). Contact depth (hitter's usual depth removed) on the previous pitch's flight time, with fixed effects for every cell of pitcher, pitch type, season and the previous pitch's outcome kind (taken for a ball, taken for a strike, fouled, missed), so a previous pitch is compared only with previous pitches of the same outcome before the same pitch of the same pitcher; the count as twelve categories, the pitch number, the previous pitch's side, height and zone, the pitch's own flight and location, platoon. Variants: cells also split by the previous pitch's family (fastball, breaking, offspeed), to see whether the speed or the type is what hitters key on; alternations (previous pitch of another family) and repeats separately. Misses on 2026 swings: speed-follow (TIMING-02) and the contact window (CONTACT-03) alone and together. Checked on synthetic plate appearances first: TIMING-02's design leaked a planted after-swing effect into after-take slopes when the sample was split (alternations +0.18, repeats -0.24 with nothing planted after takes), because the after-swing shift differed by pitch type; with cell fixed effects every after-take slope is within 0.06 of zero in the planted world and within 0.03 in the null, and the planted after-swing slope is recovered at -0.66 to -0.71 | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run.** Predictions: (1) after a taken ball and after a called strike the previous pitch's flight slope is positive, +0.04 to +0.15 inches per 10 ms with the interval excluding zero, in both seasons; (2) after a miss it is negative with the interval excluding zero in both seasons; (3) within the previous pitch's family the after-take slopes fall by at least half (hitters key on the type they just saw, not its exact speed); (4) alternations and repeats reported, no prediction; (5) speed-follow and the window correlate positively, and speed-follow gains at least 0.05 nats per 1,000 swings beyond the window with the interval excluding zero. Decision rule: if (1) and (2) hold, both sequence effects are frozen (this code) and confirmed once on the untouched set (TIMING-03F), and the scouting reading is stated (after a take hitters meet the next pitch as if expecting the other speed; a missed swing's timing persists); if (1) fails, TIMING-02's after-take result was the design's artifact. If (5) holds, speed-follow is a hitter trait candidate for its own frozen test; if not, it is a facet of the contact window. Results (run 37883288815; 195,396 contacts in 2025, 138,932 in 2026 through July): slope of contact depth on the previous pitch's flight, inches per 10 ms, with cell fixed effects and full controls: **after a called strike +0.122 [0.097, 0.144] (2025) and +0.135 [0.098, 0.172] (2026); after a taken ball +0.036 [0.021, 0.055] and +0.033 [0.014, 0.056]; after a foul -0.018 and +0.015 (zero); after a miss -0.186 [-0.230, -0.145] and -0.222 [-0.278, -0.159].** With cells also split by the previous pitch's family, the after-take slopes vanish (called strike +0.034 [-0.026, +0.108] and -0.029; ball 0.000 and -0.029) while after a miss stays (-0.17 and -0.33). Alternations: after a called strike +0.102 and +0.095; repeats -0.006 and -0.026. Own flight within type +1.34 and +1.26. Misses: speed-follow and window correlate 0.45; window alone gains 0.23 [0.14, 0.32], speed-follow alone 0.18 [0.09, 0.27], both 0.28 [0.18, 0.39] (speed-follow -0.106 [-0.142, -0.070] beside the window; the increment over the window, 0.053, had no interval computed). Predictions: (1) held in sign and interval for both kinds of take in both seasons, but the range failed for taken balls (0.036 and 0.033 against at least 0.04); (2) held; (3) held (the after-take slope is about the previous pitch's family, not its speed within the family); (5) the correlation held, the interval on the increment was not computed, so it is not met. **Under the rule nothing is frozen from this registration** ((1) failed its range for balls). Reading: after a hitter takes a strike, he meets the next pitch as if expecting the other kind of pitch (a slower family taken for a strike, the next pitch met earlier; a faster one, later), and after he misses, the missed swing's timing carries into his next contact; a fouled pitch carries nothing. The first effect is about pitch family, so it is an expectation of alternation, not a perceptual speed contrast. These are confirmed only by a new registration on fresh data (TIMING-03F). | after called strike +0.12-0.13; after miss -0.19 to -0.22 |
| TIMING-03F | TIMING-03's design scored once on the untouched set: every contact swing from August 1, 2026 with a previous pitch in the plate appearance (Savant), cell fixed effects for pitcher, pitch type, season and the previous pitch's outcome kind, full controls, the same variants. Code: timing_final_study in tools/brl_matchup.py, frozen in the commit that adds this row (the depth code of timing3_study restricted to the untouched rows); it refuses to run without final_eval and a frozen commit. Checked on synthetic plate appearances: planted after-swing carried timing recovered at -0.65 and -0.72 with after-take slopes within 0.03 of zero; nothing planted, every full-control slope within 0.03 of zero | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run; one scoring.** Predictions on the untouched contacts (full controls): (a) after a called strike the slope is positive, +0.05 to +0.20 inches per 10 ms of the previous pitch's flight, interval excluding zero; (b) after a miss it is negative with the interval excluding zero; (c) after a taken ball the point estimate is positive; (d) with cells split by the previous pitch's family, the after-called-strike slope is within 0.06 of zero. Decision rule: if (a) and (b) hold, both sequence effects are credited as forward-tested: after taking a strike, hitters meet the next pitch as if expecting the other family (so repeating the family after a called strike catches them early or late), and a missed swing's timing carries into the next contact; they enter the matchup engine's scouting view as sequencing notes (sizes in inches of contact depth), not the simulator, which does not play pitch sequences. If either fails, the development result is recorded as not confirmed. Results (run 37883572585, frozen commit 8795ff00; 65,356 untouched contacts with a previous pitch, August 1 to September 27, 2026): slopes in inches of contact depth per 10 ms of the previous pitch's flight, full controls: **(a) after a called strike +0.151 [0.101, 0.192]; (b) after a miss -0.239 [-0.317, -0.146]**; (c) after a taken ball +0.026 [-0.007, +0.055]; after a foul -0.008. (d) Within the previous pitch's family, after a called strike +0.087 [-0.064, +0.232], outside the predicted 0.06 of zero (its interval is wide). Alternations: after a called strike +0.064, after a ball +0.048; repeats after a miss -0.52 [-0.92, +0.04]. **(a), (b) and (c) held, (d) failed: under the rule both sequence effects are credited as forward-tested; whether hitters key on the previous pitch's family or on its speed stays open.** Stated: after a hitter takes a called strike, he meets the next pitch farther out front the slower that strike was, about 0.15 inches per 10 ms of its flight (a changeup taken for a strike against a fastball taken for a strike, roughly 0.6 inches); after he misses, he meets the next pitch later the slower the pitch he missed, about 0.24 inches per 10 ms; a fouled pitch and a taken ball carry little. These are sizes of where contact happens, small in outcomes (SEQ-01 found no gain in misses from sequence surprise); they go to the scouting view as sequencing notes. | credited on untouched data: +0.15 after called strikes, -0.24 after misses |
| CONTACT-03 | CONTACT-02's frozen miss model scored once on the untouched set: the rates model (gradient boosting on the pitch's speed, movement, location, approach angles, spin, extension, release height, count, type, platoon and arm angle, with the hitter's whiff rate and bat speed from earlier swings) plus one term, the hitter's contact-depth spread from earlier contact swings (shrunk with k = 50), its coefficient fitted on the rates model's cross-fitted logits; the recipe refitted on all development swings (2025 and 2026 before August 1, at least 30 earlier contacts) and scored on every swing from August 1, 2026 (Savant). Code: contact_final_study in tools/brl_matchup.py, frozen in the commit that adds this row; it refuses to run without final_eval and a frozen commit. Also reported: the flexible model with attack angle and spread (CONTACT-01's G2). Checked on synthetic swings first: with no spread effect the term is +0.03 per 10 inches [-0.03, +0.10] and gains -0.01; with a spread effect that is purely the hitter's level, the whiff rate absorbs it by August and the term, fitted where earlier-swing histories are thinner, over-corrects (gain -0.57), so the untouched gain is positive only if the spread carries more than a better estimate of the hitter's level | **Registered in the commit that added this row, before the run; one scoring.** Predictions: the coefficient negative with the interval excluding zero (development -0.90 per 10 inches); the gain over the rates model on the untouched swings positive with the interval excluding zero, about 0.2 nats per 1,000 swings; observed misses fall across spread quintiles beyond the rates model. Decision rule: if both hold, the contact window (spread of contact depth) is credited as a forward-tested predictor of misses beyond a hitter's whiff rate and enters the matchup engine's miss model; if the gain fails, it is recorded as a development-only result (a better early estimate of the hitter's level, not new information). Results (run 37882301605, frozen commit 618cc7f3; 534,943 development swings, 107,290 untouched swings from August 1 to September 27, 2026, whiff rate 22.7%): coefficient **-0.86 per 10 inches of spread [-0.98, -0.73]** (development -0.90); **gain over the rates model +0.16 nats per 1,000 swings [0.05, 0.27]**; the rates model's error by spread quintile runs from -0.8 points (narrowest windows, 8.3 inches: observed 22.2%, rates model 21.4%) to +0.9 (widest, 10.0 inches: 22.7% against 23.5%), and the frozen model removes it at both ends (22.3% and 22.6%). The flexible model with attack angle and spread gains +1.07 [0.66, 1.43] on the same swings. **Both predictions held on untouched data: credited.** A hitter's contact window, the spread of where along the flight he meets the ball, measured from his earlier contact swings, predicts his misses beyond his whiff rate two months ahead: hitters who meet the ball over a wider range of depths miss less than their rate says, those with narrow windows more. It enters the matchup engine's miss model. | credited on untouched data: +0.16 (spread), +1.07 (geometry) |

| DISP-01 | Game-to-game spread of runs. Replay caveat: in the replay, early-season bullpens fall back to the previous season's last ten games because no roster is available; the live system takes the bullpen from the official pregame feed, so the replay's April number (0.25415) is pessimistic for live use and has not been separated from model error.

Missing metrics mean **not measured**. Operational improvements are not model-accuracy gains. Preserve failures and all model-selection attempts. The through-2024-model/full-2025 replay has not been run; previous 2025 inspection and any repeated selection must be disclosed.

## Required entry for model experiments

Record ID and timestamp before scoring, hypothesis, source vintage/coverage, training/tuning windows, current/candidate hashes, fixed game manifest, both paired deltas and95% intervals, numerical MCSE separately, disposition/reason and later reuse of evaluation results. A model is kept only after the declared gate; otherwise preserve the current model and mark the candidate dropped/deferred. Repeated selection on2025 makes it development despite through2024 fitting.

Measurement work: complete-world team/total distributions; pregame-frozen simple pitching/NB baselines; seven paired skill rows in How close and Track record; retained batting detail; per-metric date-block report utility. These are reporting/data-contract changes, not claimed accuracy gains. The local source check used 31 earlier complete games (62 team boxes), produced 19 named pitcher comparator distributions, and changed no saved forecast. The initial live baseline's recent cache support is disclosed.

Game-balanced summaries and paired date-block report utility implemented. | Not run |
